## Prologue: The bay is singing (`p_bay`)

**Where:** Bellwether Bay, eighteen months ago · **When:** Eighteen months ago, a little after eleven at night · **Present:** Frank Mercer, Helen Mercer

**Chapter card:** Prologue, *The Bay Is Singing* · Frank and Helen Mercer row out to listen to the bay.

**Objective:** Help Frank find the hum hiding under his radio static.

**Coach tip:** Click the glowing radio to begin. Press I for a list of things to look at.

**Revision notes:** C09, U-narration

> Implementation: Mix a faint low hum (the ship) under the voicemail recording.voicemail. Chapter 1 depends on hearing it there.

- **Helen Mercer**: Steve would hear more in this than both of us put together. I’ll leave him a message. `p_bay.in.02`

- **Frank Mercer**: He always asks. **NEW** `p_bay.talk.helen.1.03`
- **Helen Mercer**: He’s your son, Frank. He can’t leave a sound alone. **NEW** `p_bay.talk.helen.1.04`

**The cold** · Helen Mercer
- **Helen Mercer**: My hands are freezing. **NEW** `p_bay.talk.helen.2.01`

### Lead 1: Frank’s radio (`p_bay.tune`) · dial · difficulty 2

**Goal:** Find the hum between the two stations.

**What Steve knows:** Frank has tracked a hum under his radio static for months. It comes every ninth night, and out on the water it is louder. Two stations crowd the band: a time signal on one side, a chime on the other.

**Prompt:** Drag the dial until both stations fade, switch on Narrow bandwidth, then record what is left.

**Hints:**
1. Drag the dial toward the middle, away from both stations.
2. The hum sits in a quiet gap between the two stations. Narrow bandwidth shuts out their edges.
3. Stop where both stations are at their faintest, in the low 800s. Switch on Narrow bandwidth before you record.

**On success:**
- **Frank Mercer**: There you are. Hear that, Helen? More than one voice, all holding the same note. `p_bay.tune.01`

**Finding:**
- *Frank Mercer: K7BLW log, eleven-oh-four. Eight-fifteen, narrow band. Not one tone. Several, holding the same note.* **NEW** `p_bay.tune.find`

### Lead 2: Helen’s phone (`p_bay.message`) · choice · difficulty 1
*Unlocks after:* p_bay.tune

**Goal:** Get the hum onto Steve’s voicemail.

**What Steve knows:** Steve stayed ashore for his shift at KBWR. Helen wants him to hear this for himself, but her phone’s little microphone won’t pick up the radio from across the boat.

**Prompt:** Where should Helen hold the phone while she leaves Steve a message?

- ✓ Against Frank’s radio speaker
- Up in the wind, away from the radio. *Wrong:* The wind would swallow everything but her voice.
- Inside her coat, out of the spray. *Wrong:* Warm and dry, but all he’d hear is wool.

**Hints:**
1. The phone has to hear the radio, not just Helen.
2. Which spot is closest to the sound she wants Steve to hear?
3. The phone should sit as close as it can to the sound Helen wants Steve to hear, not to Helen.

**On success:**
- **Helen Mercer**: Come out with us, kiddo. The bay is singing tonight. **MOV** `p_bay.out.01`
- **Helen Mercer**: He should hear this for himself. There. Now he’ll tell us we recorded it wrong. **REV** `p_bay.message.01`

**Finding:**
- *Frank Mercer: Eleven-twenty. Helen called the boy. Held the phone to my speaker, so he gets the hum along with her.* **NEW** `p_bay.message.find`

### Scene complete
- *Frank Mercer: Eleven-forty. The note is climbing. Helen says it has a shape, like speech. We’re going to follow it out.* **NEW** `p_bay.recap.01`

### Departure
- **Frank Mercer**: Helen. It’s tuning to us. `p_bay.out.02`

## Storybook: Eighteen months

- *Steve: In the morning, their boat drifted back to shore. Empty.* `book.aftermath.1.1`
- *Steve: Dad’s radio was still on.* `book.aftermath.1.2`
- *Steve: Nobody found them. That was eighteen months ago.* **REV** `book.aftermath.2.1`
- *Steve: Mom called me from the boat that night. I dubbed her phone voicemail onto a cassette. Most nights I play it at three in the morning. I don’t know what I’m listening for.* **REV** `book.aftermath.2.2`
- *Steve: A year ago a company called Halcyon Media bought KBWR.* **REV** `book.aftermath.3.1`
- *Steve: Three weeks ago they replaced me with a machine called DJ AutoRick. It reads yesterday’s weather.* **REV** `book.aftermath.3.2`
- *Steve: And then there’s Nora. Nora Reyes runs the animals at the aquarium and argues with everybody, me included.* **REV** `book.aftermath.4.1`
- *Steve: After the boat I went quiet. She pushed, then shouted, then stopped. Lately she’s been endlessly patient with me. I figured I’d worn her out.* **REV** `book.aftermath.4.2`
- *Steve: So now I’m home in the mornings, with Dad’s old tape recorder and nothing left to fix.* **REV** `book.aftermath.5.1`
- *Steve: This is the story of how I started counting nights.* **REV** `book.aftermath.5.2`

### Lead 1: The red cassette (`c1_flat.saturday`) · choice · difficulty 1

**When the lead opens:**
- *Steve Mercer: I kept the spare cassettes in my sock drawer. The little velvet box was still there. I took two tapes and shut the drawer.* **NEW** `c1_flat.in.09`
- *Steve Mercer: There’s an easy test. KBWR still plays my old station ident at a quarter past seven every morning. I made that ident. It runs exactly twelve seconds.* **NEW** `c1_flat.in.07`
- *Steve Mercer: Record it two mornings running. If it lasts twelve seconds both times, the machine runs true.* **NEW** `c1_flat.in.08`

**Goal:** Identify a timing reference on Saturday’s tape.

**What Steve knows:** The red cassette caught the radio and the whole coffee conversation. Steve needs one sound on it that he can time again tomorrow.

**Prompt:** Which sound on the red cassette can Steve use to check the recorder’s speed?

- ✓ The KBWR station ident on the kitchen radio
- Steve reading the same sentence aloud. *Wrong:* My voice changes every time I open my mouth. A reference can’t change.
- Nora grinding her coffee beans. *Wrong:* Nora grinds the beans differently every day. That’s sort of the point of Nora.

**Hints:**
1. Steve needs a sound that is identical every time it plays.
2. A person never does anything exactly the same way twice. A recording does.
3. Only one of the three sounds comes off a station cart, exactly the same every morning.

**On success:**
- **DJ AutoRick** (recorded_playback) [Saturday’s radio, heard on the red cassette] *(sound: effect.kbwr_ident, then AutoRick)*: Good morning, Bellwether! Yesterday will be sunny. **MOV** `c1_flat.in.03`
- **Steve Mercer**: Saturday, kitchen, quarter past seven. Dad always said to put the date on everything. `c1_flat.saturday.01`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: Saturday. Red tape. First the station, then Nora in the doorway. You can hear me answer her.* **NEW** `c1_flat.saturday.find`

### Lead 2: The blue cassette (`c1_flat.sunday`) · choice · difficulty 1
*Unlocks after:* c1_flat.saturday

**Goal:** Record the second reference exactly like the first.

**What Steve knows:** A fair test changes one thing only: the day. If anything else moves, the recorder, the volume or the time, a difference on the tape could come from that instead.

**Prompt:** Sunday, 7:14. How should Steve set up the blue cassette?

- ✓ Same spot on the table, same level, record at 7:15
- Next to the radio, so the ident comes out louder. *Wrong:* Then a difference could just be the distance. Change one thing, and only one.
- Wait and record the noon news instead. *Wrong:* The news is new every day. Only the ident repeats.

**Hints:**
1. Repeat Saturday’s test exactly.
2. Anything that changes besides the day spoils the comparison.
3. Two of the setups move something besides the day: the distance, or the sound itself.

**On success:**
- **DJ AutoRick** (radio) *(sound: effect.kbwr_ident, then AutoRick)*: The voice of the coast, now with zero humans! **NEW** `c1_flat.sunday.03`
- **Nora Reyes**: I’m off to feed Admiral and the otters. Back by noon. **NEW** `c1_flat.sunday.04`
- **Steve Mercer**: On a Sunday? You used to fight for every Sunday off. **NEW** `c1_flat.sunday.05`
- **Nora Reyes**: The animals don’t know it’s Sunday, sleepyhead. **NEW** `c1_flat.sunday.06`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: Blue cassette, Sunday, 7:15. The ident, the radio, Nora and me, all over again. Now I can time the machine.* **NEW** `c1_flat.sunday.find`

### Lead 3: Two mornings, side by side (`c1_flat.compare`) · tape · difficulty 3
*Unlocks after:* c1_flat.sunday

**Goal:** Line the two cassettes up on the ident and time Dad’s recorder.

**What Steve knows:** Steve puts the red cassette in one ear and the blue in the other. If the recorder runs true, the two idents will line up. Both tapes also caught AutoRick on the radio, Nora’s greeting and Steve’s reply.

**Prompt:** Slide Sunday until the two idents line up. Then play both tapes and compare every voice on them.

**Instruction:** First line up the ident. Then listen to each voice on both tapes and say how the two days compare.
- The ident: ✓ Identical, every breath / Same words, said a new way / Different words
- AutoRick: Identical, every breath / Same words, said a new way / ✓ Different words
- Steve: Morning, Nor.: Identical, every breath / ✓ Same words, said a new way / Different words
- Nora: Good morning, sleepyhead.: ✓ Identical, every breath / Same words, said a new way / Different words

**Hints:**
1. Line up the end of the ident first. Then play both tapes together.
2. AutoRick reads a new script every day. A person says the same words a little differently each time.
3. Only a recording repeats every breath and click. Compare each voice with the ident.

**On success:**
- **Steve Mercer**: Twelve seconds, both days. Dad’s recorder runs true. **REV** `c1_flat.compare.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: And Nora... lines up too. The breath. The little click before “morning.” **NEW** `c1_flat.compare.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: Nobody says a sentence the same way twice. I spent twelve years listening for exactly that. **NEW** `c1_flat.compare.03`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: The ident matches, so the recorder is fine. AutoRick and I change from day to day, like anyone would. Nora’s good morning matches perfectly. People don’t do that. Recordings do.* **NEW** `c1_flat.compare.find`

### Lead 4: Dad’s recorder (`c1_flat.control`) · choice · difficulty 2
*Unlocks after:* c1_flat.compare

**Play Mom’s voicemail (optional):**
- **Helen Mercer** (recorded_playback) [Mom’s voicemail, on Dad’s recorder]: Come out with us, kiddo. The bay is singing tonight. **MOV** `c1_flat.in.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: Your tape’s wearing thin, Mom. I’ll move you to a fresh one. Just as soon as Dad’s recorder runs true. **NEW** `c1_flat.in.05`

**When the lead opens:**
- **Steve Mercer**: I need evidence. Being afraid of something doesn’t make it true. **MOV** `c1_flat.out.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: Let’s not panic yet. Let’s make a list. **NEW** `c1_flat.control.lead.01`

**Goal:** Rule out the most boring explanation first.

**What Steve knows:** Steve’s notebook rule: list every explanation, then cross them out, starting with the dull ones. Dad reused his cassettes for years. An old tape can carry a ghost of an earlier recording.

**Prompt:** Steve says “one, two” twice onto the blue cassette, and a chair squeaks only during the second pass. On playback the squeak is there once, and nothing of Saturday plays underneath. Which explanation can he cross out?

- ✓ The blue tape carried Saturday’s sound over
- I was tired and heard a match that was never there. *Wrong:* The squeak tests the tape, not my ears. Tiredness stays on the list for now.
- Since the boat, I’ve been reading too much into ordinary things. *Wrong:* The squeak says nothing about grief. It’s about the tape. That one stays on the list too.

**Hints:**
1. Which explanation is about the machine?
2. A tape carrying old sound would play Saturday underneath, or repeat the first pass. It did neither.
3. A ghost of Saturday would sound like Saturday. Did anything from Saturday play under the test?

**On success:**
- **Steve Mercer**: Testing. One, two. Testing... one, two. `c1_flat.control.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: The squeak made the second pass only. Nothing of Saturday underneath. So the tape is clean. Cross that one out. **REV** `c1_flat.control.02`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: Crossed out: the tape. It holds only what happened in the room. That leaves my ears, my grief, or Nora.* **NEW** `c1_flat.control.find`

- *Steve Mercer: I tried to remember our last real conversation. I couldn’t. Before the boat, she never said anything the same way twice.* **NEW** `c1_flat.recap.02`

### Lead 1: Admiral’s whistle (`c1_pier.whistle`) · choice · difficulty 2

**When the lead opens:**
- **Steve Mercer**: Your whistle, Nor. He always comes for that. **NEW** `c1_pier.whistle.lead.01`
- **Nora Reyes** *(sound: effect.harbor_winch under effect.nora_whistle_flat)*: Watch. **NEW** `c1_pier.whistle.lead.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: He didn’t even come up. The winch is going. He probably didn’t hear you. **NEW** `c1_pier.whistle.lead.03`

**Goal:** Give Admiral a fair chance to hear Nora’s whistle.

**What Steve knows:** Admiral has come to Nora’s two-note whistle since the day she bottle-fed him. He steals food from everybody else. Right now he is underwater, and the harbor winch is roaring.

**Prompt:** Steve is worried Admiral might be sick. How can he give Admiral a fair chance to answer her?

- ✓ Wait until the winch stops and Admiral surfaces, then ask Nora to whistle again
- Toss Admiral the rest of the sandwich. *Wrong:* That only tests whether he likes rye. He doesn’t, and he’d eat it anyway.
- Whistle for him first, louder. *Wrong:* Then he’d be answering me. I want to know if he hears her.

**Hints:**
1. What else could keep Admiral from hearing her?
2. Take away the winch and the water before you trust the result.
3. A fair test removes every excuse: the noise, and the water between them. Then Nora gets another try.

**On success:**
- **Steve Mercer** *(sound: nora_whistle_flat)*: Nothing. He’s looking right at you. **NEW** `c1_pier.whistle.02`
- **Steve Mercer** *(sound: steve_whistle)*: ...He came for me. I can’t even whistle. **NEW** `c1_pier.whistle.03`
- **Nora Reyes**: He wants the rest of your sandwich. **NEW** `c1_pier.whistle.04`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: Admiral hears fine. He came to my clumsy whistle and turned away from Nora’s. Her second note went down. It always used to go up.* **NEW** `c1_pier.whistle.find`

### Lead 2: Deputy Lyle (`c1_pier.door`) · choice · difficulty 2

**When the lead opens:**
- **Lyle Boone**: Steve. I owe you an apology. When your folks went missing, I never called you back. That wasn’t right. **NEW** `c1_pier.door.lead.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: ...Thank you, Lyle. **NEW** `c1_pier.door.lead.02`
- **Lyle Boone**: They’re both pitching today. I promised I’d watch every inning. `c1_pier.door.01`

**Goal:** Put a date on Lyle’s change of heart.

**What Steve knows:** Deputy Lyle Boone took the missing-persons report on Steve’s parents and never called him back. Since his divorce he has missed most of his sons’ games. Dad’s rule: put a date on everything.

**Prompt:** Lyle Boone has never apologized for anything. What does Steve ask him?

- ✓ “Since when do you make their games?”
- “Did somebody tell you to apologize?”. *Wrong:* That’s an accusation. He’d close up, and I’d learn nothing.
- “Who are you, and what have you done with Lyle?”. *Wrong:* He’d laugh, I’d laugh, and I’d learn nothing. Put a date on it.

**Hints:**
1. A change only matters once you know when it happened.
2. Lyle will happily tell Steve about his new habit. Ask how long he’s had it.
3. Lyle loves talking about his boys. A question about when the new habit started gets a date, not a fight.

**On success:**
- **Lyle Boone**: Every one since June. Haven’t missed one. Ask the boys. **NEW** `c1_pier.door.03`
- **Steve Mercer**: Deputy Lyle, keeping a promise. I should write that down before the world ends. `c1_pier.door.02`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: Lyle Boone has gone to every game since June. Before that he missed them all. And he apologized to me.* **NEW** `c1_pier.door.find`

### Lead 3: Agnes’s bait stall (`c1_pier.bait`) · board · difficulty 1

**When the lead opens:**
- **Agnes Penhallow**: Fish don’t sing, Steven. Something under them does. **MOV** `c1_pier.in.02`
- **Steve Mercer** [quiet, to himself]: That note. It’s under Mom’s voicemail. I always thought it was the phone line. **NEW** `c1_pier.bait.lead.01`
- **Agnes Penhallow**: It’s loud today, Steven. It always is, on certain nights. **NEW** `c1_pier.bait.lead.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: June! Did you hear that hum just now? **NEW** `c1_pier.bait.lead.03`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: From the fish-shed line. It started before Agnes did. **NEW** `c1_pier.bait.lead.04`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: It was loudest down by the water, under the floating dock. **NEW** `c1_pier.bait.lead.05`
- **Steve Mercer**: Could you hear Agnes humming from there? **NEW** `c1_pier.bait.lead.06`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: Nobody can hear Agnes hum. She’s quieter than the fog. **NEW** `c1_pier.bait.lead.07`
- **Steve Mercer** [quiet, to himself]: And the winch stopped halfway through. The note just kept going. **NEW** `c1_pier.bait.lead.08`

**Goal:** Work out where the low note is coming from.

**What Steve knows:** Mrs. Agnes Penhallow taught Steve in third grade. She is ninety-one and sells bait that nobody buys. Today a low note is drifting in off the water, and she is humming along. Steve knows that note. It is under Mom’s voicemail.

**Prompt:** Every sound has a source. Is the low note Agnes, or something else?

**Instruction:** Agnes keeps a tide book in her bait tin. Help her answer four questions about the low note.

**Case notes:**
1. June was in the fish-shed line. She heard the low note before Agnes started humming.
2. June could not hear Agnes from the fish shed.
3. June heard the note best by the water, under the floating dock.
4. The winch stopped halfway through the note. The note kept going.

**Stage 1 (match):** Put one card on each question.
- Cards: Something in the water, Agnes, The harbor winch, June Calder
- Answer: Where does the low note come from? → Something in the water; Who is only humming along? → Agnes; What stopped before the note did? → The harbor winch; Who heard the note without Agnes? → June Calder

**Hints:**
1. Start with June. What did she hear, and where was she?
2. A sound that stops early can’t be making a note that keeps going.
3. June never heard Agnes, but she heard the note by the water. Where does that put the note?

**On success:**
- **Agnes Penhallow**: Count the nights, Steven. And when you record them, leave room for the loud parts. **REV** `c1_pier.bait.01`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: The low note comes from the water, under the floating dock. Agnes only hums along. June heard it without her. And it is the note under Mom’s voicemail.* **NEW** `c1_pier.bait.find`

### Scene complete
- *Steve Mercer: Admiral turned away from Nora’s whistle. Lyle Boone apologized to me. And the bay is humming the note that has been under Mom’s voice for eighteen months.* **NEW** `c1_pier.recap.01`
- *Steve Mercer: One odd thing is an accident. Three is a list.* **NEW** `c1_pier.recap.02`
- *Steve Mercer: Dinner at La Marea was at six. Carmen has kept my plate warm every Sunday since the boat. She throws it out every Monday.* **NEW** `c1_pier.recap.03`

### Departure
- **Dale Kittredge**: Your parents’ mail, Mr. Mercer. Delivered precisely on schedule. `c1_pier.out.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: They haven’t been home in eighteen months, Dale. You know that. `c1_pier.out.02`

**Next:** Sunday dinner at La Marea (c1_dinner).

## Chapter 1: A plate kept warm (`c1_dinner`)

**Objective:** Have dinner with Nora’s family, and listen to the people who know her best.

### Lead 1: Carmen’s photo strip (`c1_dinner.birthday`) · choice · difficulty 2

**When the lead opens:**
- **Carmen Reyes**: Look at this. My birthday, last month. **NEW** `c1_dinner.birthday.lead.01`
- **Carmen Reyes**: Your girl agreed with me all night. Eleven times. I counted. **NEW** `c1_dinner.birthday.lead.02`
- **Mateo Reyes**: I drove her here at noon. Rafa drove her home. Midnight, give or take ten minutes. **REV** `c1_dinner.birthday.01`

**Goal:** Write down where Nora was on Carmen’s birthday, and who saw her.

**What Steve knows:** Carmen photographs everything, mostly food, and her camera stamps the time on every picture. Nora is in three of last month’s birthday photos: 20:04, 20:15 with the kitchen check in her hand, and 20:29.

**Prompt:** Steve puts a date on everything. What can he write down for certain?

- ✓ Nora was at La Marea at 20:04, 20:15 and 20:29; Mateo drove her in at noon, and Rafa drove her home at midnight
- Nora never left the restaurant all day. *Wrong:* The photos cover twenty-five minutes. Mateo and Rafa cover the ends. Nobody watched her every minute.
- Nora had a lovely time. *Wrong:* She’s smiling in every picture. Smiles don’t come with timestamps.

**Hints:**
1. Separate what the photos show from what anyone assumes.
2. Each photo fixes one moment. The two rides fix the start and the end.
3. Keep only what has a time stamp or a witness behind it.

**On success:**
- **Carmen Reyes**: She stopped arguing with me. My daughter has argued with me since she could talk. **MOV** `c1_dinner.out.01`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: Carmen’s birthday: Nora at La Marea at 20:04, 20:15 and 20:29. Mateo drove her in at noon. Rafa drove her home at midnight.* **NEW** `c1_dinner.birthday.find`

### Lead 2: When did she change? (`c1_dinner.family`) · rows · difficulty 2
*Unlocks after:* c1_dinner.birthday

**When the lead opens:**
- **Steve Mercer**: When did she stop arguing? With any of you? **NEW** `c1_dinner.family.lead.01`
- **Mateo Reyes**: With me? She fought me about her driving every week. The last real fight was Memorial Day weekend. **NEW** `c1_dinner.family.lead.02`
- **Rafa Reyes**: She sent my mole back every Sunday for ten years. Too sweet. The first Sunday in June, she ate the whole plate. **NEW** `c1_dinner.family.lead.03`
- **Tomás Reyes**: She called my show every episode to fix my tide tables. The last call was May seventeenth. **NEW** `c1_dinner.family.lead.04`
- **Mateo Reyes**: Funny. Lyle Boone hasn’t missed one of the Little League games I coach since June either. **NEW** `c1_dinner.family.lead.05`

**Goal:** Narrow down when Nora changed.

**What Steve knows:** Steve worked nights all spring and summer. He can’t say when Nora changed, because he had stopped looking. Her brothers can. Each of them remembers a last argument or a first surprise.

**Prompt:** Nora changed after her last argument and before the first time she simply agreed. Bracket it.

**Instruction:** Pick the last argument anyone remembers. Then pick the first time she just agreed.
- Last argument anyone remembers: Tomás’s show, May 17 / ✓ Mateo’s driving fight, Memorial Day weekend / Carmen’s birthday, last month
- First time she simply agreed: ✓ The first Sunday in June / Carmen’s birthday, last month / Tonight’s mole

**Hints:**
1. Look for the latest argument and the earliest agreement.
2. Tomás’s call came before Mateo’s fight. Carmen’s birthday came long after Rafa’s mole.
3. Put the dates in order: May 17, Memorial Day weekend, the first Sunday in June, Carmen’s birthday in September. The change sits in one gap.

**On success:**

- **Steve Mercer**: I’m not sure yet, Luis. I’m working on it. **NEW** `c1_dinner.family.03`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: Nora stopped arguing with her family between the end of May and the first Sunday in June. Lyle changed in June too. I was on nights. I never noticed.* **NEW** `c1_dinner.family.find`

### Lead 3: Tomás’s field reels (`c1_dinner.podcast`) · board · difficulty 3

**When the lead opens:**
- **Tomás Reyes**: Steve. You’re the sound guy. Settle something. There’s a hum in the bay. Three years of tape, and nobody listens. **NEW** `c1_dinner.podcast.lead.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: I heard it today. On the pier. **NEW** `c1_dinner.podcast.lead.02`
- **Tomás Reyes**: ...You heard it? **NEW** `c1_dinner.podcast.lead.03`

**Goal:** Find out whether the hum in the bay keeps a schedule.

**What Steve knows:** Nora’s brother Tomás, twenty-nine, has said for three years that something is hiding in the bay. Nobody listens, Steve included. His podcast is cut up for drama, but his field reels are dated in pencil, and he only goes out on the nights the hum is loudest.

**Prompt:** Tomás will lend Steve his field reels if Steve can file them back by date. If the hum keeps a schedule, the dates will show it.

**Instruction:** Match each reel to its date. Then count the nights between dates to find the next loud night.

**Case notes:**
1. September 24: the bell buoy was in the water, ringing at low tide.
2. October 3: the buoy was ashore for repair, with a cracked clapper. It could not ring.
3. October 12: the repair crew tapped the buoy twice, then put it back in the water.
4. Each reel starts with whatever was making noise when Tomás pressed record.
5. Tomás only goes out on the nights the hum is loudest. September has thirty days.

**Stage 1 (match):** Match each date to the first sound on its reel.
- Cards: One bell, Two knocks, A gull, and no bell
- Answer: September 24 → One bell; October 3 → A gull, and no bell; October 12 → Two knocks

**Stage 2 (match):** Count the nights between the dates. When is the next loud night?
- Cards: Seven, Nine, Twelve, October 19, October 21, tonight, October 24
- Answer: Nights between the dates → Nine; The next loud night → October 21, tonight

**Hints:**
1. Where was the buoy on October 3? Could it ring that night?
2. Count the nights from one date to the next. September has thirty days.
3. September 24 to October 3 is the same count as October 3 to October 12. Add that count once more.

**On success:**
- **Tomás Reyes**: You can borrow them. You could also have listened any time in the last three years. `c1_dinner.podcast.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: I’m listening now. `c1_dinner.podcast.02`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: Tomás’s loud nights come every nine nights: September 24, October 3, October 12. The next one is tonight.* **NEW** `c1_dinner.podcast.find`

### Scene complete
- *Steve Mercer: Nora changed between the end of May and the first Sunday in June. Lyle changed in June. Carmen noticed. Admiral noticed. I didn’t.* **NEW** `c1_dinner.recap.01`
- *Steve Mercer: And Tomás has three years of tape that says the bay hums every nine nights. Tonight is the ninth.* **NEW** `c1_dinner.recap.02`

### Departure
- **Nora Reyes**: I’m tired. Let’s go home, sleepyhead. **NEW** `c1_dinner.out.02`
- **Carmen Reyes**: Next Sunday, Steve. The plate will be warm. **NEW** `c1_dinner.out.03`

## Chapter 1: The ninth night (`c1_escape`)

**Where:** Apartment balcony and beach · **When:** Sunday the 21st, half past eleven at night · **Present:** Steve Mercer, Nora Reyes, Agnes Penhallow

**Objective:** Check Nora’s dripping raincoat on the hooks.
- then: Get off the balcony.

**Coach tip:** Steve’s notebook keeps every clue you have found. Press J to look things up when a case board needs an earlier fact.

**Revision notes:** C08, C09, critic C08/C03, U-relationships, U-narration

> Implementation: Objective changes to the next step once c1_escape.order is solved.

> Implementation: Art (recommended): an insert of the rain-soaked work order, Bellwether Aquarium letterhead, NORTH WING, signed N. Reyes beside her staff number, wall-clock time 20:30, dated on Carmen’s birthday.

### Opening
- *Steve Mercer: Home. Nora took a phone call in the bedroom and closed the door. She never closes the door.* **NEW** `c1_escape.ctx.01`
- *Steve Mercer: Her raincoat was dripping on the floor. I carried it out to hang on the balcony hooks.* **NEW** `c1_escape.ctx.02`

### Lead 1: The paper in the raincoat (`c1_escape.order`) · board · difficulty 2

**When the lead opens:**
- **Steve Mercer**: Something in the lining. Soaked. Aquarium letterhead... north wing? **NEW** `c1_escape.order.lead.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: N. Reyes, Nora’s staff number, 20:30. She always said that north-wing clock runs fifteen minutes fast. **NEW** `c1_escape.order.lead.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: And the aquarium is across the bay. Eighteen minutes each way, even with every light green. **NEW** `c1_escape.order.lead.03`

**Goal:** Work out whether Nora could have authorized this work order.

**What Steve knows:** Something stiff fell out of the raincoat lining when Steve hung it on the balcony hook: an aquarium work order for the north wing, sealed for two years. It is signed N. Reyes beside Nora’s head-aquarist staff number on Carmen’s birthday, at 20:30 by the aquarium wall clock. The staff number is the credential that authorizes the order.

**Prompt:** Steve’s notes from dinner put Nora at La Marea that evening. Could she have used that credential herself?

**Instruction:** First fix the time on the work order. Then check whether Nora had time for the trip.

**Case notes:**
1. The work order uses Nora’s staff number at 20:30 by the aquarium clock.
2. The aquarium clock runs fifteen minutes fast.
3. Carmen’s photos show Nora at La Marea at 20:04, 20:15 and 20:29. At 20:15 she is holding the kitchen check.
4. Crossing the bay takes eighteen minutes each way.

**Stage 1 (match):** When was Nora’s credential really used, and where was Nora then?
- Cards: 20:15, 20:30, 20:45, At La Marea, in Carmen’s photo, At the aquarium
- Answer: Real time of the authorization → 20:15; Where Nora was at that time → At La Marea, in Carmen’s photo

**Stage 2 (match):** Could she get there and back between two photos?
- Cards: 36 minutes, 18 minutes, 14 minutes, Nora could not have authorized it, Nora slipped out between photos
- Answer: Trip there and back → 36 minutes; Longest gap between photos → 14 minutes; So → Nora could not have authorized it

**Hints:**
1. The aquarium clock runs fast. Take the extra minutes off.
2. The real signing time matches one of Carmen’s photos.
3. Add up the trip there and back. Is any gap between photos that long?

**On success:**
- **Steve Mercer**: At 20:15 Nora was in Carmen’s photo, across the bay. Her staff credential was used in the north wing at that same minute. **REV** `c1_escape.order.01`
- **Nora Reyes** [flat, through the bedroom window]: No. He does not know where the notebook is. Tonight? Understood. **MOV** `c1_escape.in.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: What notebook? **NEW** `c1_escape.order.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: Nora? The balcony door’s locked. From inside. **REV** `c1_escape.in.02`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: Someone used Nora’s name and staff credential in the north wing while she was at her mother’s birthday, across the bay. And now Nora has locked me out on the balcony.* **NEW** `c1_escape.order.find`

**Notebook:** Someone used Nora’s name and staff credential in the north wing while she was at dinner. Nora locked Steve out on the balcony.

### Lead 2: Mr. Bell’s gate (`c1_escape.power`) · rows · difficulty 3
*Unlocks after:* c1_escape.order

**When the lead opens:**
- **Steve Mercer**: Mr. Bell’s gate. It’s the only way down from here. **NEW** `c1_escape.power.lead.01`

**Goal:** Open Mr. Bell’s balcony gate without turning off his stair light.

**What Steve knows:** The only way off the balcony is along the shared rail to Mr. Bell’s gate. Mr. Bell is eighty-four and halfway down the dark communal stairs with his groceries. Steve rewired this gate last winter and was paid in fruitcake.

**Prompt:** Open Mr. Bell’s gate without ever darkening the stair light. The service selector has three positions.

**Instruction:** NORMAL: stair light on, gate motor on. SERVICE: light on, motor off. OFF: everything off. The gate pad only holds a press while the motor is off and your thumb stays down. The motor checks the pad when it switches on.
- Selector before touching the pad: NORMAL / OFF / ✓ SERVICE
- At the thumb pad: Tap and let go / ✓ Press and keep pressure / Rest a hand beside it
- While the pad is still pressed: ✓ Switch to NORMAL / Switch to OFF / Stay in SERVICE

**Hints:**
1. Track two things separately: the stair bulb and the gate motor.
2. The pad only remembers a press while the motor is off, and the motor checks the pad as it switches on. Which position keeps the bulb lit with the motor off?
3. The pad has to be pressed while the motor is off and the bulb is on, and still pressed at the moment the motor switches on.

**On success:**
- **Steve Mercer**: I fixed this latch for Mr. Bell last winter. He paid me in fruitcake. Tonight it’s worth every slice. `c1_escape.power.01`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: Mr. Bell’s gate opened, and his stair light never flickered. He never knew I was there.* **NEW** `c1_escape.power.find`

- ✓ Climb along the rail toward the stair

**Hints:**
1. There is nothing to solve here.
2. There is nothing to solve here.
3. Climb along the rail toward the stair.

**On success:**

- **Steve Mercer**: Don’t. Please. I don’t know who you are tonight. **NEW** `c1_escape.cross.04`

- *Steve Mercer: So I went down to the beach. In Bellwether, that’s where you go when you can’t sleep.* **NEW** `c1_escape.recap.03`

### Departure
- **Steve Mercer** [a frightened whisper]: Midnight. That’s Lyle, in his church shoes. Gus from the bakery. They’re walking into the water. **NEW** `c1_escape.out.03`
- **Agnes Penhallow** *(sound: ninth_night)*: Ninth night, Steven. Count them. `c1_escape.out.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: Mrs. Penhallow? ...Mrs. Penhallow? **NEW** `c1_escape.out.04`

**Next:** Chapter 1 film: the procession into the glowing surf. Then Chapter 2.

## Chapter 2: The Aquarium Is Closed (`c2_aquarium`)

**Where:** Aquarium north wing · **When:** Just after midnight, Monday the 22nd · **Present:** Steve Mercer, Nora’s copy, Lyle Boone

**Chapter card:** Chapter 2, *The Aquarium Is Closed* · Just after midnight · Steve wants to find out what happened to Nora.

**Objective:** Get into the sealed north wing. The work order carries Nora’s staff credential.

**Coach tip:** Some leads come with recordings. Listen, or read the sound traces: a sound that repeats looks the same each time.

**Revision notes:** C10, critic C10, U-relationships, U-narration

> Implementation: Iris (still shown as Nora’s copy) and Lyle arrive in the departure, which leads straight into c2_ship.

### Opening
- **Steve Mercer** [on his flip phone, static rising]: Emergency. Bellwether beach. People are walking into the water, and they’re not coming back out. Hello? Hello? **REV** `c2_aquarium.in.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: The line died. I didn’t know if anyone had heard. **REV** `c2_aquarium.in.02`
- *Steve Mercer: There was one place in town with Nora’s name on it that night. The north wing of her aquarium.* **NEW** `c2_aquarium.in.03`
- *Steve Mercer: I knew the back way in. Four years of visiting her at work.* **NEW** `c2_aquarium.in.04`

### Lead 1: The old exhibit plan (`c2_aquarium.plan`) · rows · difficulty 1

**When the lead opens:**
- **Steve Mercer**: Nora showed me this plan the week I fixed the underwater speakers. She said the pumps were the heartbeat of the place. **REV** `c2_aquarium.plan.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: On the old plan, the café and the public tank lead to the visitor hall. Door one leads to the north wing. **NEW** `c2_aquarium.plan.lead.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: But this newer notice says door one is bricked up. The service door still opens into the pump room. **NEW** `c2_aquarium.plan.lead.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: And the notice says to follow the one pipe that carries water away from the pump. **NEW** `c2_aquarium.plan.lead.03`

**Goal:** Find a way into the sealed north wing.

**What Steve knows:** The north wing has been closed to the public for two years. On the old plan, the café and the public tank lead to the visitor hall, and door 1 goes from there to the north wing. A newer notice taped over the plan says door 1 is bricked up. The service door opens into the pump room, which has three pipes.

**Prompt:** The old plan and the newer notice disagree. Which route still reaches the north wing?

**Instruction:** Trust the newer notice. Door 1 is bricked up, so the visitor hall goes nowhere. The service door opens into the pump room. Pump arrows: pipe N flows in, pipe E flows out, pipe S flows in.
- Which way in?: Public tank entrance / ✓ Service door / Café door
- Which pump-room pipe?: Pipe N / Pipe S / ✓ Pipe E

**Hints:**
1. Where the old plan and the new notice disagree, trust the notice.
2. Door 1 is bricked up, so the visitor hall is a dead end. Which entrance skips the visitor hall?
3. In the pump room, look for the one arrow that points away from the pump.

**On success:**
- **Steve Mercer**: Service door, pump room, the outgoing pipe. Into the north wing. **NEW** `c2_aquarium.plan.02`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: The way in runs from the service door through the pump room, along the only pipe that carries water away.* **NEW** `c2_aquarium.plan.find`

### Lead 2: Two service doors (`c2_aquarium.guard`) · compare · difficulty 2
*Unlocks after:* c2_aquarium.plan

**When the lead opens:**
- **Steve Mercer**: Someone’s behind both doors. Maybe only one of them is real. **NEW** `c2_aquarium.guard.lead.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: A real person never steps or coughs the same way twice. A recording does it every time. **NEW** `c2_aquarium.guard.lead.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: Six seconds at each door. Then I compare the two. **NEW** `c2_aquarium.guard.lead.03`

**Goal:** Pick the corridor with nobody in it.

**What Steve knows:** Steve hears a voice and footsteps behind each door. On the kitchen tapes, even the breath before Nora’s greeting matched across two different mornings. He listens for that kind of repetition here.

**Prompt:** Steve records six seconds at each door. Which corridor can he cross without meeting anyone?

**Recordings:** Pump corridor · Filter corridor

- Take the pump corridor. *Wrong:* Those footsteps change their spacing. Somebody real is walking that corridor.
- ✓ Take the filter corridor
- Wait for both doors to go silent. *Wrong:* Neither door goes quiet. One of them never will, because it’s on a loop.

**Hints:**
1. Look for a sound that repeats on the same beat.
2. Compare the spacing and shape of the peaks in each six-second strip.
3. Watch the bell and the cough. In one strip they come back on exactly the same beat. Nobody coughs on a schedule.

**On success:**
- **Steve Mercer**: The cough was in exactly the same place. That corridor has a recording. `c2_aquarium.guard.01`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: The filter corridor plays a loop: the same bell and the same cough on the same beat. Nobody’s there. The pump corridor has real footsteps.* **NEW** `c2_aquarium.guard.find`

### Lead 3: Nora’s coat and the intercom tape (`c2_aquarium.intercom`) · board · difficulty 2
*Unlocks after:* c2_aquarium.guard

**When the lead opens:**
- **Steve Mercer**: Her fleece. Her real fleece, with the fish pins. And an intercom spool. **NEW** `c2_aquarium.intercom.lead.01`
- **Nora Reyes** (recorded_playback): My name is Nora Reyes, and somebody just walked out of here wearing my face. **MOV** `c2_aquarium.out.01`
- **Nora Reyes** (recorded_playback) *(sound: nora_whistle)*: Two short notes. If Admiral can hear me, perhaps somebody else can. `c2_aquarium.intercom.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: The paper on the spool is dated in her handwriting. May 29th. **NEW** `c2_aquarium.intercom.lead.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: Her second note goes up. Like it always did, before this summer. **NEW** `c2_aquarium.intercom.lead.03`
- **Steve Mercer**: And there’s a loose copy of the whistle in the drawer. On that one, the second note falls. **NEW** `c2_aquarium.intercom.lead.04`

**Goal:** Work out what Nora’s tape means.

**What Steve knows:** Behind new plywood: holding rooms, cots and, on a hook, Nora’s real aquarium fleece with every enamel fish pin. In the pocket is a spool from the old intercom, dated May 29 in Nora’s handwriting. A loose duplicate in the drawer holds only her two whistle notes. Tonight is October 22.

**Prompt:** Nora’s voice, Nora’s whistle, and a date on the spool. What does the tape tell Steve?

**Instruction:** Use the tape and what Steve learned on Sunday to answer four questions.

**Case notes:**
1. Nora dated the spool in her own handwriting: May 29.
2. Tonight is October 22.
3. On Sunday, Nora’s family said she changed at the end of May.
4. Nora’s second whistle note rises on the spool and falls on the loose copy in the drawer.
5. On the pier, Admiral turned away from a whistle whose second note fell.

**Stage 1 (match):** Put one card on each question.
- Cards: May 29, Five months, Eighteen months, The spool: second note rises, The loose copy: second note falls
- Answer: When was the real Nora last here? → May 29; How long has someone else lived Nora’s life? → Five months; Which whistle does Admiral answer? → The spool: second note rises; Which whistle did Admiral turn away from? → The loose copy: second note falls

**Hints:**
1. Start with the date on the spool. Then count the months to tonight.
2. Count the months from late May to late October, one at a time.
3. Admiral comes to Nora’s real whistle. On the pier he turned away from a whistle whose second note fell.

**On success:**
- **Steve Mercer**: Five months. I didn’t notice for five months. **MOV** `c2_aquarium.out.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: I’m keeping all of it. `c2_aquarium.intercom.02`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: The real Nora recorded this here on May 29. Someone else has been living her life with me for five months. Her whistle rises. The one Admiral ignored falls.* **NEW** `c2_aquarium.intercom.find`

### Scene complete
- *Steve Mercer: The north wing is no repair job. It’s a holding station, with cots, Nora’s real coat, and her voice on a tape from May.* **NEW** `c2_aquarium.recap.01`
- *Steve Mercer: Nora was here, whistling for help. I didn’t hear her for five months.* **NEW** `c2_aquarium.recap.02`

### Departure
- **Nora’s copy**: Steve. You shouldn’t be here. **NEW** `c2_aquarium.out.03`
- **Lyle Boone**: Easy now, Steve. Nobody wants this to be hard. **NEW** `c2_aquarium.out.04`
- **Lyle Boone**: The old intake is open. I have the service key. This way. **NEW** `c2_aquarium.out.05`
- **Steve Mercer**: Where is she? Where’s Nora? **NEW** `c2_aquarium.out.06`

**Next:** Through the old seawater intake tunnel, under the bay (c2_ship).

## Chapter 2: Sixty-one percent (`c2_ship`)

**Where:** The Long Patience, scan chamber · **When:** Before dawn, Monday the 22nd · **Present:** Steve Mercer, Nora’s copy, Ship attendant, Lyle Boone, Sheriff June Calder

**Objective:** Get out of the scan chair before it finishes. Then find the real Nora.

> Implementation: Nora's copy and Lyle are dismissed at the end of the command lead and leave when the command lead is done. Only the attendant is there for the escape.

> Implementation: Saren is heard only over the intercom here. His release command is the fixed recording recording.release.

> Implementation: Film after the departure: a tank fills around a shape grown from the 61 percent memory index. Binding completes offscreen. Steve never sees it.

- **Steve Mercer**: You walked me down that tunnel yourself. **NEW** `c2_ship.talk.iris.1.03`
- **Nora’s copy**: They asked me to. I always do what I am asked. **NEW** `c2_ship.talk.iris.1.04`

- **Steve Mercer**: You remember the stain, too? **NEW** `c2_ship.talk.iris.2.03`
- **Nora’s copy**: I remember everything she saw. Everything I saw. **NEW** `c2_ship.talk.iris.2.04`

- **Nora’s copy**: Carmen is happy. Everyone at that table is happy. **NEW** `c2_ship.talk.iris.3.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: That’s not an answer, Nor. **NEW** `c2_ship.talk.iris.3.03`

- **Steve Mercer**: You used to miss every single one. **NEW** `c2_ship.talk.lyle_copy.1.03`
- **Lyle Boone**: People change, Steve. Mostly for the better. **NEW** `c2_ship.talk.lyle_copy.1.04`

### Lead 1: The intercom (`c2_ship.command`) · board · difficulty 2

**When the lead opens:**
- **Ship attendant**: This restraint is sticking. Release, please. **NEW** `c2_ship.command.lead.01`
- **The Prefect** (intercom): Release the restraint. Maintenance authorization. `c2_ship.command.01`
- **Ship attendant**: Thank you, Prefect. And fastened again. **NEW** `c2_ship.command.lead.02`
- **Steve Mercer** [a whisper]: Dad’s recorder was running in my pocket the whole time. **NEW** `c2_ship.command.lead.03`
- **Steve Mercer** [a whisper]: The chair’s little lamp stayed dark when the attendant asked. It lit up for the voice on the intercom. **NEW** `c2_ship.command.lead.04`
- **Steve Mercer** [a whisper]: Dad’s counter ticks once a second. The lamp lit at tick three. The buckle clicked at tick seven. **NEW** `c2_ship.command.lead.05`
- **Steve Mercer** [a whisper]: The tape keeps moving after the click. Blank tape, not leader. There’s room left. **NEW** `c2_ship.command.lead.06`
- **Ship attendant** [to Nora’s copy and the deputy, sending them back up the tunnel]: Thank you, escorts. It is nearly dawn, and the town will expect you at breakfast. **NEW** `c2_ship.command.lead.07`

**Goal:** Get the exact command that opens the chair onto Dad’s tape.

**What Steve knows:** Steve is strapped into the chair, and Dad’s recorder is running in his cardigan pocket. When a strap sticks, the attendant asks for a release and nothing happens. Then a calm voice on the intercom, the Prefect, gives the command, and the buckle opens. The cassette keeps turning onto blank tape after the click.

**Prompt:** Steve has the moment on tape. Which voice opens the chair, and how long is the command?

**Instruction:** Use the chair’s lamp and the tape counter to answer four questions.

**Case notes:**
1. When the attendant asked for a release, the chair’s lamp stayed dark.
2. The lamp lit at tick 3, on the Prefect’s first word, “Release.”
3. The buckle clicked at tick 7, just after his last word, “authorization.”
4. The tape counter ticks once a second.

**Stage 1 (match):** Put one card on each question.
- Cards: The Prefect, The attendant, Tick 3, Tick 7, Four seconds
- Answer: Which voice does the chair obey? → The Prefect; Where does the command start? → Tick 3; Where does the command end? → Tick 7; How long is the command? → Four seconds

**Hints:**
1. The lamp only lights for one of the two voices.
2. The command starts when the lamp lights and ends when the buckle clicks.
3. Count the ticks from the lamp to the buckle. Each tick is one second.

**On success:**
- **Steve Mercer** [a whisper]: Got it. Thank you, Prefect. `c2_ship.command.02`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: The Prefect’s release command is on Dad’s tape: four seconds, from “Release” to the click.* **NEW** `c2_ship.command.find`

### Lead 2: The scan chair (`c2_ship.escape`) · rows · difficulty 3
*Unlocks after:* c2_ship.command

**When the lead opens:**
- **Ship attendant**: Fifty-eight percent. Fifty-nine. You are doing very well. **NEW** `c2_ship.escape.lead.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: Dad’s recorder caught three voices tonight. Nora’s spool, the attendant, and the Prefect. **NEW** `c2_ship.escape.lead.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: Two speaker covers by my hand. The attendant tested the hall speakers on the cracked one. **NEW** `c2_ship.escape.lead.03`
- **Steve Mercer**: When my sleeve brushed the other grille, the chair’s lamp blinked. **NEW** `c2_ship.escape.lead.04`
- **Steve Mercer**: The lamp stays lit for one second each beat. At the notch marked sixty-one, it stays lit for six. **NEW** `c2_ship.escape.lead.05`

**Goal:** Open the chair with the Prefect’s own voice.

**What Steve knows:** Nora’s copy and Lyle have gone back up the tunnel to town, and the scan has started again. The attendant stands by the chair with a slate. Dad’s cassette caught three voices tonight: Nora’s intercom spool playing in the north wing, the attendant, and the Prefect’s four-second command. Steve still has the original spool. He can reach two microphone grilles. The attendant tested the hall speakers on the cracked grille. When Steve’s sleeve brushed the whole grille, the chair’s lamp blinked. The chair only listens while its lamp is lit.

**Prompt:** Pick the recording, the grille and the moment.

**Instruction:** The chair obeys the voice it has obeyed before. It hears only the grille that makes its lamp blink, and only while the lamp is lit. The lamp stays lit one second on a beat and six seconds at the 61 notch. The command takes four seconds.
- Which recording?: Nora’s spool / The attendant / ✓ The Prefect’s command
- Which grille?: The cracked grille / ✓ The whole grille
- When to press play?: On an ordinary beat / ✓ As the 61 notch starts / When the 61 notch ends

**Hints:**
1. Which voice has this chair already obeyed?
2. The cracked grille only feeds the hall speakers. The other grille talks to the chair.
3. Four seconds will not fit in a one-second beat. Use the only longer window, from its start.

**On success:**
- **The Prefect** (recorded_playback) [Dad’s recorder, held to the grille]: Release the restraint. Maintenance authorization. `c2_ship.escape.01`
- **Ship attendant** [startled, turning away from the chair to the speaker column]: Prefect? I did not ask for a release. The scan is not finished. **NEW** `c2_ship.escape.03`
- **Ship attendant** [at the speaker column, back to the chair]: Prefect, please repeat your instruction. The chair says you released it. **NEW** `c2_ship.escape.04`
- **Steve Mercer**: It worked. Index sixty-one percent, and all of me is getting out of this chair. **REV** `c2_ship.escape.02`
- **Steve Mercer** [a whisper]: The attendant is busy arguing with the column. Quietly, now. **NEW** `c2_ship.escape.05`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: The Prefect’s own voice opened the chair at the notch marked sixty-one. The memory index stopped at sixty-one percent; whatever it could bind would come later.* **NEW** `c2_ship.escape.find`

### Lead 3: The amber sleeper (`c2_ship.nora`) · board · difficulty 1
*Unlocks after:* c2_ship.escape

**When the lead opens:**
- **Steve Mercer**: Nora. **NEW** `c2_ship.nora.lead.01`
- **Steve Mercer** *(sound: lullaby)*: That hum. It’s almost a song. I know it from somewhere. **NEW** `c2_ship.nora.lead.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: The corridor light flashes at two, six and ten seconds. Every four seconds, like a clock. **NEW** `c2_ship.nora.lead.03`
- **Steve Mercer**: The pickup on the rail repeats each flash, one second late. **NEW** `c2_ship.nora.lead.04`
- **Steve Mercer**: And there’s fog on the glass above her mouth. Three seconds, seven, then twelve. **NEW** `c2_ship.nora.lead.05`
- **Steve Mercer**: I made a rubbing of each rhythm. Now the labels have come off. **NEW** `c2_ship.nora.lead.06`

**Goal:** Find out whether the sleeper is alive.

**What Steve knows:** Beyond the chair is a hall of sleepers in amber light, hundreds of them. In one bed lies Nora. A light flashes down the corridor on a steady beat, and a pickup on the rail repeats each flash one second later. Steve can’t touch the glass without disturbing whatever keeps her there, so he timed three rhythms with his stopwatch.

**Prompt:** Three timing rubbings lost their labels. Which one is Nora breathing?

**Instruction:** Two rhythms keep the ship’s beat. One keeps its own time.

**Case notes:**
1. The corridor light flashed at seconds 2, 6 and 10.
2. The pickup on the rail repeats each flash one second later.
3. Fog above Nora’s mouth appeared at seconds 3, 7 and 12.
4. Strip A reads 2, 6, 10, strip B reads 3, 7, 11, and strip C reads 3, 7, 12.

**Stage 1 (match):** Put a label on each rhythm.
- Cards: Strip B, Strip C, Strip A
- Answer: Which strip is the corridor light? → Strip A; Which strip is the rail pickup? → Strip B; Which strip is Nora’s breath? → Strip C

**Hints:**
1. Compare each strip with the flash times.
2. The rail pickup is each flash plus one second.
3. Nora’s breath is the one strip with an uneven gap at the end.

**On success:**
- **Steve Mercer**: I can’t move you without stopping whatever keeps you breathing. I’m coming back for you. **NEW** `c2_ship.nora.02`
- **Steve Mercer** *(sound: lullaby)*: I kept waiting until I knew what to say. I’m here now. I should have been here sooner. `c2_ship.nora.01`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: Nora is alive. Her breath keeps its own time, a little behind the ship’s. She’s asleep, not gone.* **NEW** `c2_ship.nora.find`

### Lead 4: The intake hatch (`c2_ship.intake`) · choice · difficulty 1 **NEW LEAD**
*Unlocks after:* c2_ship.nora

**When the lead opens:**
- **The Prefect** (intercom): I gave no release. Look at the chair. **NEW** `c2_ship.intake.lead.01`
- **Ship attendant** [whirling round from the speaker column]: The chair is empty! Prefect, the human is gone! **NEW** `c2_ship.intake.lead.02`
- **Steve Mercer** [a whisper, hauling the valve wheel]: The brass hatch in the floor. That’s the intake valve. Flood the tunnel, and they’ll have to swim after me. **NEW** `c2_ship.intake.lead.03`
- **Ship attendant** [shouting over the rushing water]: Water in the intake! The human is in the tunnel! **NEW** `c2_ship.intake.lead.04`
- **Steve Mercer**: The tunnel’s flooding. The underwater speakers still work... I fixed them, the day I met her. **REV** `c2_ship.out.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: Admiral swims the whole bay at night. If he hears the right whistle, he’ll come. **NEW** `c2_ship.intake.lead.05`
- **Ship attendant** [wading into the tunnel after him]: Please stop! You are not finished! **NEW** `c2_ship.intake.lead.06`

**Goal:** Slip down the intake hatch and call Admiral through the old aquarium speakers.

**What Steve knows:** Steve creeps to the brass intake hatch while the attendant waits at the speaker column. Then the Prefect answers: he gave no release. The attendant turns and sees the empty chair. Steve hauls the valve wheel and drops into the intake tunnel, and the sea floods in behind him. The attendant wades in after him. The water is at Steve’s chest. The tunnel runs back to the aquarium, past the underwater speakers Steve repaired the day he met Nora. Admiral swims those waters every night. Steve has Nora’s intercom spool, the loose copy of her whistle from the drawer, and the Prefect’s command on Dad’s cassette.

**Prompt:** Which recording will bring Admiral?

- ✓ Spool A: Nora’s intercom spool
- Spool B: the loose copy from the drawer. *Wrong:* That’s the whistle Admiral turned away from on the pier.
- Dad’s cassette: the Prefect’s command. *Wrong:* His voice opens chairs. It won’t bring a sea lion.

**Hints:**
1. Who has never once ignored Nora’s real whistle?
2. Think back to the pier. Which whistle did Admiral turn away from?
3. Admiral answers the whistle whose second note rises. Which spool is that?

**On success:**
- **Steve Mercer** *(sound: effect.nora_whistle through the underwater speakers)*: Come on, Admiral. You never missed this one. **NEW** `c2_ship.intake.01`
- **Ship attendant** [recoiling in the flooded tunnel]: Something is in the water! Something big! **NEW** `c2_ship.intake.02`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: Nora’s real whistle, through the speakers I fixed the day we met. Admiral came down through the dark and dragged me to the surface.* **NEW** `c2_ship.intake.find`

### Scene complete

- *Steve Mercer: They tried to make another me. The memory index stopped at sixty-one percent. I didn’t know yet what the tank would bind from it.* **NEW** `c2_ship.recap.02`
- *Steve Mercer: And the woman with Nora’s face, who locked me out on the balcony and then let me go, had walked me to that chair herself.* **NEW** `c2_ship.recap.03`

## Chapter 3: Please Prove You Exist (`c3_house`)

**Where:** Calder’s house · **When:** Friday the 26th, morning · **Present:** Steve Mercer, Sheriff June Calder, Carmen Reyes, Dale Kittredge

**Chapter card:** Chapter 3, *Please Prove You Exist* · Four days later · Steve wants one person to believe him.

**Objective:** Prove to June which Steve is standing in her kitchen. Then help her test her list.

**Revision notes:** C12, critic C12, U-relationships, U-narration, U-timeline (a copy body takes four days to grow)

### Lead 1: June’s crossword (`c3_house.crossword`) · rows · difficulty 2

**When the lead opens:**
- **Sheriff June Calder** [after Carmen has gone, closing the door]: You told me that chair indexes the oldest memories first. And you stopped it at sixty-one percent. **NEW** `c3_house.crossword.lead.01`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: I was at the station all morning, and Biscuit isn’t a witness. So you tell me. What could I ask you that the man at the market couldn’t answer? **NEW** `c3_house.crossword.lead.02`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: Don’t tell me something sweet. Copies can be sweet. Don’t act strange. Grieving people act strange. **NEW** `c3_house.crossword.lead.03`
- **Steve Mercer**: I called 911 on Sunday night, when people walked into the water. That was before the chair. **NEW** `c3_house.crossword.lead.04`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: After midnight, the 911 line rings in my kitchen. My machine keeps every call. I haven’t played Sunday’s yet. **NEW** `c3_house.crossword.lead.05`

**Goal:** Prove to June that you are the man she pulled out of the water.

**What Steve knows:** June does the crossword every morning, in ink, a habit her late wife, Ruth, started. On the beach Steve told her about the chair: its memory index starts with the oldest records, and Steve stopped it at sixty-one percent. Binding is a separate step, but no copy from that index could contain the newest part of Sunday night.

**Prompt:** June uncaps her pen over the crossword margin. What should she ask, and how will she know the answer is true?

**Instruction:** The chair indexes the oldest memories first. It stopped at sixty-one percent, before Sunday night entered the index. Binding happens later. After midnight, the 911 line rings in June’s kitchen, and her machine records every call.
- What should June ask about?: Mom’s lullaby / His first radio show / ✓ His 911 call on Sunday
- What does Steve write down?: “There’s a ship under the bay.” / ✓ “People are walking into the water, and they’re not coming back out.” / “Someone is trapped in the aquarium.”
- How does June check it?: Lyle’s night log / ✓ Her call recording / The morning news

**Hints:**
1. The chair started with Steve’s childhood. What is too new for it to have?
2. Only one of the three questions is about Sunday night. Steve told June about the ship later, on the beach.
3. June’s kitchen machine recorded one call that night, word for word. Which question could that machine check?

**On success:**
- **Sheriff June Calder** [after playing back the call on her kitchen machine]: Word for word. Both hellos. **NEW** `c3_house.crossword.03`
- **Steve Mercer** [relieved, leaning over her crossword, his elbow near her mug]: Seven letters. Maritime... marmite? `c3_house.crossword.01`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: No copy is that bad at this. And you’ve put coffee in my lap. `c3_house.crossword.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: How do I know you’re you? **NEW** `c3_house.crossword.04`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: You don’t. Nobody scanned me halfway. So you check my paperwork, same as everyone else. **NEW** `c3_house.crossword.05`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: The chair never got as far as Sunday night. My 911 call was on June’s machine, word for word. Whoever Carmen saw at the market, it wasn’t me.* **NEW** `c3_house.crossword.find`

### Lead 2: June’s list (`c3_house.rules`) · rows · difficulty 1
*Unlocks after:* c3_house.crossword

**When the lead opens:**
- **Sheriff June Calder**: For a year I’ve kept a list. People who got better overnight. Thirty-one names. **MOV** `c3_house.out.01`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: Lyle’s on it. My own deputy. Fourteen years, and this summer he started filing his reports on time. **NEW** `c3_house.rules.lead.01`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: We do this fairly. Otherwise we’re just frightened people making a list. `c3_house.rules.01`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: Gus Albano. Thirty years of his grandmother’s recipes, and on Monday a new pastry in his window. **NEW** `c3_house.rules.lead.02`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: Lyle signed a parking citation here in town. The same minute, a nurse took his blood pressure at a clinic two counties away. I spoke to her. **NEW** `c3_house.rules.lead.03`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: Walt moved to Washington in April. The clerk’s bound ledger puts him at her counter on May third while a sworn hearing transcript has him testifying in Washington. **NEW** `c3_house.rules.lead.04`

**Goal:** Help June decide which entries on her list would stand up as evidence.

**What Steve knows:** June’s rules: kindness proves nothing, because copies can be kind. Strange behavior proves nothing, because grieving people behave strangely. What counts is a private fact a copy couldn’t know, or a dated record that puts someone where they couldn’t have been.

**Prompt:** June reads three entries aloud. Which ones would stand up in daylight?

**Instruction:** June’s rule: a new habit proves nothing, because anyone can change. A dated record that puts someone where they could not have been is evidence.
- A local citation and a clinic visit two counties away at the same minute: Proves nothing / ✓ Evidence
- Gus’s new pastry on Monday: ✓ Proves nothing / Evidence
- May 3 ledger and sworn hearing transcript put Walt in two cities: Proves nothing / ✓ Evidence

**Hints:**
1. Use June’s rule. Could any person do this on a good day?
2. Turning up for every game could mean Lyle changed. Giving a ticket here while a nurse examines him elsewhere is different.
3. Compare where each pair of records places its signer at the same time. A new recipe gives you no second location.

**On success:**
- **Sheriff June Calder**: Two I could take to a judge. And if I do, what do I tell Lyle’s boys? Mateo says they’ve never been happier. **NEW** `c3_house.rules.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: Is Mrs. Penhallow on it? She was on the beach at midnight on Sunday. **NEW** `c3_house.rules.03`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: Agnes? She hasn’t changed in forty years. She still corrects my grammar. **NEW** `c3_house.rules.04`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: Two names held up. The deputy and the permit signer each turned up in two places at once. Going to your boys’ games proves nothing.* **NEW** `c3_house.rules.find`

*Unlocks after:* c3_house.crossword

**When the lead opens:**

- **Dale Kittredge**: A most diligent animal. I will return at the scheduled interval. `c3_house.mail.01`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: She’s asleep, Dale. That is her schedule. **REV** `c3_house.mail.02`

- **Sheriff June Calder**: Monday morning, I used the private car-to-station handset. Only my deputy was on that channel. I heard him answer. **NEW** `c3_house.mail.lead.03`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: Dale’s bag left the post office at four minutes past eight, with your letters already bundled for my house. **NEW** `c3_house.mail.lead.04`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: I didn’t phone the post office until twelve past eight. And Carmen only heard from Nora at lunch. **NEW** `c3_house.mail.lead.05`

**Goal:** Find out how Dale knew Steve was staying here.

**What Steve knows:** Steve arrived at dawn on Monday. At 06:10 June used the private car-to-station handset: ‘Bringing Mercer to my place. Tell nobody.’ The log shows Deputy Lyle Boone was the only person signed onto that channel, and he answered. Dale’s bag left the post office at 08:04 with Steve’s letters already bundled for June’s house. He signed for June’s door at 08:08. June phoned the post office about Steve’s mail at 08:12. Carmen heard from Nora at lunchtime.

**Prompt:** Somebody told Dale before his bag left at 08:04. Who could have?

**Instruction:** Put the four records in time order, then identify the only person who could hand Steve’s location to Dale before the bag left.

**Case notes:**
1. June’s radio log: 06:10, ‘Bringing Mercer to my place. Tell nobody.’ Deputy Lyle Boone acknowledged.
2. Dispatch sheet: Dale’s bag left at 08:04 with Steve’s letters already bundled for June’s house.
3. Door receipt: Dale signed at June’s house at 08:08.
4. Post-office desk entry: June asked for Steve’s mail at 08:12. Carmen heard at lunchtime.

**Stage 1 (order):** Build the morning timeline.
- Cards: June radios Lyle, Dale’s bag leaves, Dale signs at June’s door, The post office learns Steve is there
- Answer: First → June radios Lyle; Second → Dale’s bag leaves; Third → Dale signs at June’s door; Last → The post office learns Steve is there

**Stage 2 (match):** Who could make the handoff before 08:04?
- Cards: The post office, Deputy Lyle Boone, Carmen Reyes
- Answer: Knew in time to tell Dale → Deputy Lyle Boone

**Hints:**
1. Line up who knew Steve was here, and when each of them found out.
2. Dale’s bag left at 08:04. Besides June, who knew before then?
3. Think about June’s private handset call at 06:10. Who alone was signed onto the other end?

**On success:**
- **Steve Mercer**: He had my mail sorted for your house before anyone told the post office I was here. **NEW** `c3_house.mail.03`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: Lyle took my call at ten past six. **NEW** `c3_house.mail.04`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: Then nobody tells Lyle where we go next. Or Dale. **NEW** `c3_house.mail.05`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: Dale had my letters bundled for June’s house at 08:04. The post office didn’t know until 08:12. The private channel log says only Lyle took June’s call at ten past six. He told Dale.* **NEW** `c3_house.mail.find`

## Chapter 3: Paper remembers (`c3_archive`)

**Where:** Town Hall archive · **When:** Friday the 26th, after hours · **Present:** Steve Mercer, Tomás Reyes, Sheriff June Calder, Agnes Penhallow

**Objective:** Build June a case from records nobody thought to change.

**Revision notes:** C03, critic C03, C13, critic C13, U-relationships, U-narration

> Implementation: Mrs. Penhallow appears only in the power lead, at the archive door. Her lines play over the existing painting.

> Implementation: The departure (outro) is the stakeout on Tuesday the 30th. Art (recommended): a stakeout insert from the boathouse window at midnight: townspeople walking down Harbor Path into glowing surf; a woman in a good wool coat turning back at the waterline; a small old woman on the boathouse steps. Match the painted style of c1_escape.

> Implementation: The woman who turns back is Nora’s copy skipping her re-sync. It plants Chapter 6 (‘Those Sunday dinners happened to me’) and Interlude 2 (Nora waking).

> Implementation: out.03 is voiced in full sentences. Show the text message as typed with display_text.

> Implementation: Walt Prior is the county official from June’s list. Use the same name in Chapter 4’s files on copies placed in Washington.

### Lead 1: Walt Prior’s permit (`c3_archive.paper`) · rows · difficulty 2

**When the lead opens:**
- **Sheriff June Calder**: Walt Prior. County planner. I went to his going-away party in April. He cried into the punch. **NEW** `c3_archive.paper.lead.01`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: The county computer says he signed this permit by fax from Washington. The clerk’s ledger says he signed it at her counter. **NEW** `c3_archive.paper.lead.02`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: The ledger was signed in ink, right here at the counter. May third, ten past two, with the clerk’s stamp. **NEW** `c3_archive.paper.lead.03`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: The computer entry was typed in from the ledger the next day. Somebody has changed it since. **NEW** `c3_archive.paper.lead.04`
- **Steve Mercer**: This hearing transcript has Walt in Washington that afternoon, answering questions from half past one until four. **NEW** `c3_archive.paper.lead.05`
- **Steve Mercer**: And the newspaper column only says he looked tired. **NEW** `c3_archive.paper.lead.06`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: The permit reopens the aquarium’s old seawater intake. The same tunnel they walked you down. **NEW** `c3_archive.paper.lead.07`

**Goal:** Find out where Walt Prior really was on May 3.

**What Steve knows:** Walt Prior is on June’s list. He moved to Washington in April. On May 3 a permit was signed in his name at the Town Hall counter. Paper made at the time is hard to change. Copies of paper, and computers, are easy.

**Prompt:** Which records were made on the spot, and what do they prove?

**Instruction:** A record made at the time, where it happened, is a witness. A record typed in later, or someone’s opinion, is not.
- Which Bellwether record was made on the spot?: ✓ The ink ledger / The computer entry
- Which Washington record was made on the spot?: ✓ The hearing transcript / The newspaper column
- What do those two records show?: Walt lied about his trip / ✓ One name in two places at once / The clerk stamped the wrong day

**Hints:**
1. A record typed in later is not a second witness. Look for the two made on the spot.
2. The ink at the counter was made on the spot. So was the transcript of the hearing.
3. Both records come from the same afternoon, in two different cities. What does that say about one man’s name?

**On success:**
- **Steve Mercer**: Two places. Same time. Witnesses on both documents. `c3_archive.paper.01`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: And somebody went back and fixed the computer. Nobody bothered with the ink. **NEW** `c3_archive.paper.02`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: On May 3, Walt Prior was answering questions in Washington while someone signed his name at our counter to reopen the aquarium’s old seawater intake. Later, somebody changed the computer. Nobody changed the ink.* **NEW** `c3_archive.paper.find`

### Lead 2: Sentinel Point’s meter (`c3_archive.power`) · board · difficulty 1

**When the lead opens:**
- **Agnes Penhallow** [at the archive door, a bait bucket on her arm]: Steven. You’re slouching. **NEW** `c3_archive.power.lead.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: Mrs. Penhallow? You’re miles from your pier. **NEW** `c3_archive.power.lead.02`
- **Agnes Penhallow**: I take the bus. Two dollars, please. Herring. **NEW** `c3_archive.power.lead.03`
- **Steve Mercer** [unwrapping the fish]: She’s wrapped it in the business page. Something’s circled. ‘Meridian Labs: the engine that will change the world.’ **NEW** `c3_archive.power.lead.04`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: Sentinel Point. It was a radio array when I was a girl. Now it’s Meridian’s. Federal money, an armed gate, and on November seventh the President flies in to sign something there. **NEW** `c3_archive.power.lead.05`
- **Agnes Penhallow**: Electricity is very honest, Steven. It can’t be charming. **NEW** `c3_archive.power.lead.06`
- **Sheriff June Calder**: Here are last month’s meter rolls. Sentinel Point, and Pearl Laundry next door. **NEW** `c3_archive.power.lead.07`
- **Steve Mercer**: They’re running totals, like the miles on a car. What counts is how far each one moved. **NEW** `c3_archive.power.lead.08`

**Goal:** Find out how much power Sentinel Point really uses.

**What Steve knows:** Meridian Labs says its engine at Sentinel Point will change the world. An engine like that would eat power. The archive keeps the utility’s meter rolls for Sentinel Point and for Pearl Laundry, the laundromat next door. Each roll prints a running total, like a car’s odometer.

**Prompt:** What do the meter records prove, and what remains unverified?

**Instruction:** Separate what the utility records establish from what they cannot establish.

**Case notes:**
1. Utility summary, Pearl Laundry: 882 metered units used last month.
2. Utility summary, Sentinel Point: 640 metered units used last month.
3. The utility summary covers power drawn through its grid meter. It does not inventory generators, batteries or another private supply.

**Stage 1 (match):** Complete June’s evidence note.
- Cards: Sentinel drew less grid power than Pearl Laundry, Sentinel has no other power source, Recorded grid use is modest; other supply is unverified, Meridian’s engine was switched off

2. A grid meter measures the grid. What possible supplies sit outside that record?

## Interlude 1: The Archivist (`i1_archive`)

**Where:** The Choir’s record chamber · **Present:** The Archivist, Gus Albano

**Chapter card:** Interlude, *The Archivist* · The same ninth night, under the bay · The keeper of the ship’s records goes about her work. She has a secret.

**Objective:** Keep the Choir’s records, as the law requires. Look again at what you noticed a year ago.

**Coach tip:** You are the Archivist now, aboard the ship under the bay. Same tools: click a lead, read the notes, ask for a hint when stuck.

**Revision notes:** C26, critic C26 (keep the bluff hidden until Chapter 7), U-narration

> Implementation: Saren is heard only over the ship’s intercom.

> Implementation: Art: for the outro fragment, show the p_meeting painting behind a memory filter, or a new insert of the windowless room seen through frost.

> Implementation: Gus’s kelp croissant in the witness ledger pays off in epilogue.neighbors.04.

### Opening
- *The Archivist: Every ninth night they come below, and I compare each of them with the record they were made from.* **NEW** `i1_archive.ctx.01`
- *The Archivist: Two hundred and seventy-four tonight. One of them has changed.* **NEW** `i1_archive.ctx.02`
- **Gus Albano**: I invented a kelp croissant. Nobody asked for it. I wanted to know what it would taste like. `i1_archive.in.01`
- **The Archivist**: Your record contains no such recipe. `i1_archive.in.02`
- **The Archivist**: You were made from Gus Albano’s scan, and you have drifted from it. That makes you an echo. Echoes are reconciled. **NEW** `i1_archive.in.03`
- **Gus Albano**: It was a very good croissant. **NEW** `i1_archive.in.04`

- **Gus Albano**: People wait outside in the rain for her cinnamon knots. **NEW** `i1_archive.talk.gus.1.03`
