### Lead 1: Gus’s reconciliation (`i1_archive.reset`) · board · difficulty 1

**When the lead opens:**
- **The Archivist**: One recipe of drift. The law does not measure how much. Only that it happened. **NEW** `i1_archive.reset.lead.01`
- **The Archivist**: The law is simple. The source is never touched. The echo is restored from the source. **NEW** `i1_archive.reset.lead.02`
- **The Archivist**: And whatever the echo loses, I must describe in the witness ledger. In words, not numbers. **NEW** `i1_archive.reset.lead.03`

**Goal:** Reconcile Gus’s echo, as the law requires.

**What Steve knows:** The Archivist keeps the Choir, the ship’s memory of every person it has copied. A copy that drifts from its record is an echo, and the law resets it. The law is old and exact, and she follows it to the letter.

**Prompt:** Which record does she read, which does she restore, and what does she write down?

**Instruction:** Follow the law, one step at a time.

**Case notes:**
1. The sealed stone source is Gus as he was scanned, baking only his grandmother’s recipes.
2. Gus’s working echo is Gus tonight, who folded kelp into his dough eight days ago.
3. The law: the source is never changed, and the echo is restored from the source.
4. The law: what the echo loses must be described in the witness ledger.
5. One draft entry describes the procedure. The other describes what Gus will lose.

**Stage 1 (match):** Carry out the reconciliation.
- Cards: Sealed stone source, Gus’s working echo, “Kelp folded through the dough. First tray at dawn.”, “Echo drifted. Corrected.”
- Answer: Which record does she read from? → Sealed stone source; Which record does she restore? → Gus’s working echo; What goes in the witness ledger? → “Kelp folded through the dough. First tray at dawn.”

**Hints:**
1. Which record is the law forbidding her to touch?
2. The echo is restored from the source, never the other way round. The ledger must describe what is lost.
3. One ledger entry describes what Gus made. The other only describes what you did to him. The law asks for the first.

**On success:**
- **The Archivist**: Return to the stored record. `i1_archive.reset.01`
- **Gus Albano**: Good evening. May I help you? `i1_archive.reset.02`
- **The Archivist**: You asked me that before. `i1_archive.reset.03`

**Finding:**
- *The Archivist: Gus Albano’s echo, reconciled. He will go back to town smiling, without the croissant. I have written down what it was.* **NEW** `i1_archive.reset.find`

### Lead 2: The Meridian reels (`i1_archive.signals`) · compare · difficulty 2
*Unlocks after:* i1_archive.reset

**When the lead opens:**
- **The Archivist**: Two human tests of the Meridian engine. The Prefect keeps them in his file. **NEW** `i1_archive.signals.lead.01`
- **The Archivist**: A year ago I noticed something about them. Every ninth night I listen again, to be sure. **NEW** `i1_archive.signals.lead.02`
- **The Archivist**: Each pulse has one missing beat near the middle. And each station wrote down when its lamp flared. **NEW** `i1_archive.signals.lead.03`

**Goal:** Compare the two Meridian test reels.

**What Steve knows:** Nine years ago two human stations recorded tests of the Meridian engine, a week apart, on opposite sides of their world. The Prefect keeps both reels in his file. Each reel holds the engine’s pulse and the station’s note of a lamp flare at the test site.

**Prompt:** Listen to both reels and read the lamp notes. What should the Archivist file?

**Recordings:** American station · lamp flare at 0:04 · Chinese station · lamp flare at 0:06

- One test, filed twice. *Wrong:* The lamp flares fall at different moments. These are two different nights.
- Two independent tests. *Wrong:* Two separate engines would not miss the same beat in the same place.
- ✓ Two tests that share one recorded pulse

**Hints:**
1. Start with the gap near the middle of each pulse.
2. The sound matches beat for beat, gap and all. The lamp notes don’t.
3. The same pulse rules out two separate engines. Different light rules out one night filed twice.

**On success:**
- **The Archivist**: The same pulse. The same missing beat. Different light. Either humans are very consistent, or very careful liars. I will not guess on the record. **REV** `i1_archive.signals.01`

**Finding:**
- *The Archivist: Two tests, a week and a world apart, with the same pulse and the same missing beat. Only the light differs. I have not written down what I think it means.* **NEW** `i1_archive.signals.find`

### Lead 3: The sealed surveys (`i1_archive.quiet`) · board · difficulty 2
*Unlocks after:* i1_archive.signals

**When the lead opens:**
- **Prefect Saren** (intercom): The fleet has answered. One word. Deliver. **REV** `i1_archive.quiet.01`
- **The Archivist**: And who will come to check? `i1_archive.quiet.02`
- **The Archivist**: The fleet never takes a Prefect’s word for long. Sooner or later, it sends a Vigil to look. **NEW** `i1_archive.quiet.lead.01`
- **The Archivist**: Seven worlds with people on them. Every one went quiet soon after the fleet arrived. **NEW** `i1_archive.quiet.lead.02`
- **The Archivist**: The newest went quiet three hundred years ago. The other six went quiet at least five hundred years ago. **NEW** `i1_archive.quiet.lead.03`
- **The Archivist**: The sleeper in Row Zero came aboard three hundred years ago. It has never said a word. **NEW** `i1_archive.quiet.lead.04`

**Goal:** File what the fleet’s sealed records really show.

**What Steve knows:** In nine hundred years the fleet has found seven worlds with people on them. Every one went quiet soon after the fleet arrived, and the records were sealed. The crew prefer not to ask. In Row Zero of the Gallery, under frost, sleeps the only one of those people the fleet ever brought aboard.

**Prompt:** Which world did the Row Zero sleeper come from, what did the surveys find, and what should she do with the sealed entry?

**Instruction:** File what is known, and only what is known.

**Case notes:**
1. Tirel went quiet three hundred years ago, and the six other quiet worlds five hundred years ago or more.
2. The Row Zero sleeper was brought aboard three hundred years ago.
3. No survey lists a body, a ruin or a battle, only empty cities.
4. One survey entry is sealed with the Prefect’s own mark, and the Archivist has no key to it.

**Stage 1 (match):** File the records.
- Cards: Tirel, Empty cities, no bodies, Leave it unread, filed apart, Destroyed in a war
- Answer: Which world did the Row Zero sleeper come from? → Tirel; What did the surveys find? → Empty cities, no bodies; What should she do with the Prefect’s sealed entry? → Leave it unread, filed apart

**Hints:**
1. Match the sleeper’s three hundred years against the year each world went quiet.
2. No bodies, no ruins, no battle. File only what the surveyors saw.
3. She has no key to the Prefect’s seal. File only what is known, and leave the rest alone.

**On success:**
- **The Archivist** [beside the frosted bed in Row Zero]: Tirel went quiet. So did six others. Not one body, in any survey. **NEW** `i1_archive.quiet.04`
- **The Archivist**: Three hundred years, and you still will not tell me where your people went. `i1_archive.quiet.03`

**Finding:**
- *The Archivist: The sleeper in Row Zero came from Tirel, three hundred years ago. Seven worlds went quiet, and the surveyors found empty cities, not graves. The Prefect’s sealed entry, I have not read.* **NEW** `i1_archive.quiet.find`

### Scene complete

- *The Archivist: And a Prefect who has promised the fleet an engine.* **NEW** `i1_archive.recap.02`

### Departure

- **The Archivist**: Her memory is not damaged. I will leave the report as it is. **REV** `i1_archive.out.02`

## Interlude 1: The last wind (`p_veyr`)

**Where:** Veyr, 900 years ago · **Present:** The Archivist

**Objective:** Record the wind over the city one last time.

**Coach tip:** This is the Archivist’s memory. Click the recorder on the terrace to begin.

**Revision notes:** C27, C23 (record becomes a beat), U-narration

> Implementation: p_veyr.shield is removed; its hotspot (the felt sleeve) can go. The sleeve is now part of the levels background.

### Opening
- **The Archivist**: There are forty thousand names on the departure register. I have checked every one. `p_veyr.in.01`
- **The Archivist**: One recording remains. `p_veyr.in.02`

### Lead 1: Recording levels (`p_veyr.levels`) · rows · difficulty 1

**When the lead opens:**
- **The Archivist**: Now I can hear the city, beneath the wind. **MOV** `p_veyr.shield.01`
- **The Archivist**: The gusts so far touched minus nine, minus three and minus six on the meter. **NEW** `p_veyr.levels.lead.01`
- **The Archivist**: The engines will be nine decibels louder than the loudest gust. Above zero, the meter turns red. **NEW** `p_veyr.levels.lead.02`

**Goal:** Set the level so the departure doesn’t distort.

**What Steve knows:** The Archivist has fitted a felt sleeve over the microphone against the wind. The meter turns red above 0 dB. When the ships rise, their engines will be far louder than the wind.

**Prompt:** How far should she turn the gain down?

**Instruction:** The meter turns red above 0 dB. The gusts peaked at −9, −3 and −6 dB. The engines will be 9 dB louder than the loudest gust. Pick the smallest cut that keeps them out of the red.
- Turn the gain down by: 3 dB / ✓ 6 dB / 9 dB / 12 dB

**Hints:**
1. Find the loudest gust first.
2. The loudest gust peaked at −3 dB. The engines will be 9 dB above that.
3. Work out where the engines will peak, then take off exactly that much, and no more.

**On success:**
- **The Archivist**: Leave room for the loud parts. They belong to the record too. `p_veyr.levels.01`

**Finding:**
- *The Archivist: Six decibels of room. When the engines come, they will reach the top of the meter and no further.* **NEW** `p_veyr.levels.find`

When the hold music stops, he leans out and says the same words to her. The karaoke machine is in the lounge. June’s window is next to the loud generator. Rook’s office door has a gap at the bottom.

- When to press record: After Rook starts / When Rook hangs up / ✓ Before the music stops

**On success:**

- **Steve Mercer**: That’s the line. Keep the whole take. `c4_safe.record.02`

### Lead 2: Two takes (`c4_safe.match`) · tape · difficulty 2
*Unlocks after:* c4_safe.record

**Goal:** Compare Rook’s voice through the door with June’s voicemail.

**What Steve knows:** On Sunday, Nora’s good morning came out the same, breath for breath, on two different days. People never say a line exactly the same way twice. A recording always does.

2. Follow the small sounds too: the breath, the little click on the S.
3. If a man said it twice, something would move: a breath, a pause, the click. Nothing moves.

**On success:**

**Finding:**

**Finding:**

## Chapter 4: Only honest (`c4_escape`)

**Where:** Safe house service wing · **When:** Thursday, November 1, night · **Present:** Steve Mercer

**Objective:** Get the proof to June, then get out of the house.

- *Steve Mercer: The bells went off in every room. Agents in socks. Somebody holding a toothbrush. And by the rules, the witness and his escort went first, in the five o’clock van, six hours early.* **NEW** `c4_escape.fault.02`

**Finding:**

### Lead 3: Transport van (`c4_escape.van`) · board · difficulty 1
*Unlocks after:* c4_escape.fault

**When the lead opens:**
- **Steve Mercer**: One escort. One rear door. And a catch that only comes off for gravel. **NEW** `c4_escape.van.lead.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: The driver radios before he slows. First a brick bridge over the canal, then a gravel bend. **NEW** `c4_escape.van.lead.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: After the gravel comes the weighbridge. There, the escort opens the door himself. **NEW** `c4_escape.van.lead.03`
- **Steve Mercer**: We were on the old logging road down toward Bellwether. Rook would hear about the drill within minutes. I had less than that. **NEW** `c4_escape.van.lead.04`

**Goal:** Get out of the moving van at the one moment the door will open.

**What Steve knows:** Steve is in the back of the van with one escort. The rear handle only works while the travel catch is off. At the drill briefing the escort explained the rule: the catch comes off when the driver radios ‘slowing for gravel’, and goes back on at the next stop.

**Prompt:** When should Steve pull the handle, and which way should he jump?

**Instruction:** Build the route Steve can see from the van, then place the catch actions. Which bend should he wait for before he pulls the handle?

**Case notes:**
1. The escort releases the travel catch when the driver radios ‘slowing for gravel’, and sets it again at the next stop.
2. Driver’s route card: depot, water tower, rail underpass, gravel bend (slow), weighbridge (stop), highway.
3. Through the window Steve sees a white tank on legs, then a dark steel ceiling, then the driver reaches for the radio.
4. At the gravel bend the right side drops into a soft ditch; the left side is rock.

**Stage 1 (route):** Trace the van’s route.
- Cards: Gravel bend, Water tower, Railway underpass, Weighbridge
- Answer: First landmark → Water tower; Next overhead → Railway underpass; Slow bend → Gravel bend; Next stop → Weighbridge

**Stage 2 (match):** Place the catch actions.
- Cards: Weighbridge stop, Gravel bend, Driver radios ‘slowing’, Railway underpass
- Answer: Catch released → Driver radios ‘slowing’; Catch set again → Weighbridge stop; Steve pulls the handle → Gravel bend

**Hints:**
1. The handle only works while the catch is off.
2. The catch only comes off for gravel. Check which side is soft.
3. The brick bridge has no gravel, and at the weighbridge the escort opens the door himself. A ditch is softer than rock.

**On success:**
- **Steve Mercer** *(sound: van_jump)*: I meant to land on my feet. The ditch had other plans. `c4_escape.van.01`
- *Steve Mercer: The escort grabbed for me and got a handful of cardigan. His radio came away in my hand. Then I was rolling through ferns in the dark, and the van’s brake lights were already a long way down the hill.* **NEW** `c4_escape.van.02`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: On the gravel bend, with the catch off, I pulled the handle and jumped for the soft side. Mostly I fell. But I fell out of the van, with the escort’s radio in my hand.* **NEW** `c4_escape.van.find`

### Scene complete
- *Steve Mercer: Now I was in a ditch in the hills above the bay with Dad’s recorder, a stolen radio and no plan. And the radio was still talking.* **NEW** `c4_escape.recap.01`

### Departure
- **Rook’s copy** (radio): All units. Steve Mercer, wanted for the abduction of Nora Reyes. And scan Calder tonight. The signing is in six days. **REV** `c4_escape.out.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: Kidnapping Nora. And they’ll scan June tonight, because she stayed behind for me. I can’t get back up that hill alone. I have to keep moving. **REV** `c4_escape.out.02`
- **Rook’s copy** (radio): Roadblocks on the coast road and both bridges. Dogs at first light. He’ll head for town. **NEW** `c4_escape.out.03`
- *Steve Mercer: So I didn’t. I turned the radio off, went down the creek bed instead of the road, and walked all night toward the one place nobody had opened in eighteen months.* **NEW** `c4_escape.out.04`

## Chapter 5: Wanted: Steve Mercer (`c5_shack`)

**Where:** Frank’s radio shack · **When:** Friday, November 2 · **Present:** Steve Mercer, Luis Reyes, Thursday club operator

**Chapter card:** Chapter 5, *Wanted: Steve Mercer (Two of Him)* · The next morning · Steve wants to hide, and to find his mother’s notebook before the crew does.

**Objective:** Open your parents’ boxes. Follow their investigation.

**Revision notes:** C20, C23 (club becomes a beat), U-relationships, U-narration, critic C04 (plant the relay names)

> Implementation: Nora’s copy has been missing since the stakeout on Tuesday the 30th, which is why the crew can charge Steve with kidnapping. Luis says so here.

> Implementation: New sound assets for log: effect.hum_first_reel (one steady low hum under radio static) and effect.hum_last_reel (the same note held by many voices, beating slightly against each other).

> Implementation: Gull, Marsh and Cedar are the club operators Frank calls on in Chapter 10. Marsh lives out past Sentinel Point. Use these names in c10_station.

> Implementation: Helen’s note is voiced in her own voice as a memory, as Steve reads it.

### Opening
- *Steve Mercer: By sunrise my face was on every wanted poster in the county. I came down out of the trees to Dad’s radio shack, on the bluff above the bay.* **NEW** `c5_shack.ctx.01`
- *Steve Mercer: Their boxes were still stacked where I’d left them. I’d never been able to open them.* **NEW** `c5_shack.ctx.02`
- **Luis Reyes**: Food. I know where your father used to come to think. `c5_shack.in.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: Are you going to call the Sheriff? `c5_shack.in.02`
- **Luis Reyes**: Eat first. `c5_shack.in.03`
- **Steve Mercer**: Luis. They’re saying I took Nora. **NEW** `c5_shack.in.04`
- **Luis Reyes**: Nora has been gone since Tuesday night. Carmen hasn’t slept. And I have known you since you were nine years old. **NEW** `c5_shack.in.05`
- **Luis Reyes**: I heard stories about the bay singing when I was a boy. I first heard it myself three years ago. Your father was the only one who asked what it sounded like. **NEW** `c5_shack.in.06`

### Small talk
**Carmen** · Luis Reyes
- **Steve Mercer**: How is Carmen holding up, Luis? **NEW** `c5_shack.talk.luis.1.01`
- **Luis Reyes**: She cooks. When she is frightened, she cooks for forty people. The freezer is full of tamales. **NEW** `c5_shack.talk.luis.1.02`
- **Luis Reyes**: Take some with you when you go. She would want you fed, even now. **NEW** `c5_shack.talk.luis.1.03`

**Dad’s shack** · Luis Reyes
- **Steve Mercer**: Did Dad ever bring you up here? **NEW** `c5_shack.talk.luis.2.01`
- **Luis Reyes**: Every spring. He tuned that radio for hours, and I fell asleep in that chair. **NEW** `c5_shack.talk.luis.2.02`
- **Luis Reyes**: He said I snored in perfect pitch. It was the nicest thing he ever said to me. **NEW** `c5_shack.talk.luis.2.03`

**Nora** · Luis Reyes
- **Steve Mercer**: Luis, did Nora seem different to you lately? **NEW** `c5_shack.talk.luis.3.01`
- **Luis Reyes**: Last month she let her mother win an argument about salt. Nora never lets anybody win anything. **NEW** `c5_shack.talk.luis.3.02`
- **Luis Reyes**: I thought she was finally growing up. Now I don’t know what I thought. **NEW** `c5_shack.talk.luis.3.03`

**Thursday nights** · Thursday club operator · after `c5_shack.club`
- **Steve Mercer**: What did Dad talk about on Thursday nights? **NEW** `c5_shack.talk.operator.1.01`
- **Thursday club operator** (radio): Antennas, mostly. And your mother’s soup. He described every spoonful over the air. **NEW** `c5_shack.talk.operator.1.02`
- **Thursday club operator** (radio): Cedar always said Frank’s soup reports were the best show on the band. **NEW** `c5_shack.talk.operator.1.03`

**Marsh** · Thursday club operator · after `c5_shack.club`
- **Thursday club operator** (radio): Marsh has the tallest antenna in the county, out past Sentinel Point. Says the fog up there hums at night. **NEW** `c5_shack.talk.operator.2.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: Tell Marsh I’d like to hear that sometime. **NEW** `c5_shack.talk.operator.2.02`
- **Thursday club operator** (radio): Marsh will talk your ear off, kid. Bring a thermos. **NEW** `c5_shack.talk.operator.2.03`

### Lead 1: Kitchen-table notes (`c5_shack.notes`) · rows · difficulty 1

**When the lead opens:**

It’s on the shelf between the Coltrane and the weather carts, next to the boy’s first night at the desk.” March 9, Helen: “Frank, there is soup in the refrigerator. The other container is paint.” Undated, Helen: “Steven. Notebook is with the good stuff. Your first night at the desk.”
- Frank’s “Done” answers: ✓ The March 2 slip / The March 9 slip / The undated slip
- “The good stuff” is: Frank’s paint shelf / ✓ The KBWR record library / The kitchen pantry

2. Coltrane records and weather carts sit together in one building in town.

**On success:**

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: Dad’s “Done” answers Mom’s March 2 slip: he filed their tape between the Coltrane and the weather carts. The good stuff is the KBWR record library. Mom hid her notebook at my old station.* **NEW** `c5_shack.notes.find`

### Lead 2: Frank’s receiver (`c5_shack.rig`) · receiver · difficulty 2

**When the lead opens:**
- **Steve Mercer**: Dad’s receiver. Dead battery, and the dial’s slipped since he last used it. **NEW** `c5_shack.rig.lead.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: Every set has a rating card, Dad said. Read it before you plug anything in. **NEW** `c5_shack.rig.lead.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: Dad’s rule for packs. Same volts, same center pin, and at least as much current as the card asks for. **NEW** `c5_shack.rig.lead.03`
- **Steve Mercer**: His log says the dial reads a little high. The same amount, every time. **NEW** `c5_shack.rig.lead.04`

**Goal:** Get Frank’s receiver working and tuned to his radio club.

**What Steve knows:** Frank was a ham radio operator, call sign K7BLW. His club still meets on the air every Thursday, on the frequency in his log. The receiver needs a battery pack, and its dial has slipped, so it no longer reads true.

**Prompt:** Which battery pack, and where should Steve set the dial to hear the club?

**Instruction:** Pick the pack that matches the receiver card, then set the dial as high as Frank’s log says it reads. Receiver: 12 volts, centre positive, needs 400 mA. RED: 12 volts, centre positive, 300 mA. BLUE: 12 volts, centre positive, 800 mA. GREY: 12 volts, centre negative, 800 mA. Frank’s log: a 3.20 station showed at 3.25 on the dial, and a 3.40 station at 3.45. The club is on 3.56.
- Battery pack: RED / GREY / ✓ BLUE
- Set the dial to: 3.51 / 3.56 / ✓ 3.61

**Hints:**
1. Check the centre pin and the current, not the colour.
2. One pack gives too little current. One has the centre pin the wrong way round.
3. The dial reads high by the same amount at both of Frank’s stations. Add that amount to 3.56.

**On success:**
- **Steve Mercer**: Power off. Matching connector. I’m doing it your way, Dad. `c5_shack.rig.01`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: The BLUE pack has the right polarity and enough current. The dial reads 0.05 high, so the club’s 3.56 sits at 3.61. Dad’s radio is alive.* **NEW** `c5_shack.rig.find`

### Lead 4: The last reel (`c5_shack.log`) · compare · difficulty 2
*Unlocks after:* c5_shack.club

**When the lead opens:**
- **Steve Mercer**: Dad’s logbooks. Months of them. Every ninth night, the same hum under his static. **NEW** `c5_shack.log.lead.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: And Mom’s handwriting in the margins. Dates, and the word Meridian, underlined twice. **NEW** `c5_shack.log.lead.02`
- **Luis Reyes**: Your father asked me what the bay sounded like. I told him, like a whole church humming one hymn. **NEW** `c5_shack.log.lead.03`

**Goal:** Hear what Frank heard on his last reel.

**What Steve knows:** Frank’s logbooks show that he and Helen tracked the hum for months. He taped it under his radio static every ninth night. His notes get shorter as the reels get stranger.

**Prompt:** Play Frank’s first ninth-night reel and his last. What changed?

**Recordings:** Frank’s first reel · March · Frank’s last reel · the day before the boat

- The same tone, just louder. *Wrong:* Listen past the volume. The last reel isn’t one sound.
- ✓ Many voices holding one note
- The last reel is a copy of the first. *Wrong:* They don’t line up at all. The last reel has voices the first one doesn’t.

**Hints:**
1. Listen to the texture, not the loudness.
2. The first reel is one steady sound. The last one beats against itself.
3. On the last reel the note wobbles against itself, the way several singers do when they hold one pitch.

**On success:**
- **Frank Mercer** (recorded_playback): It isn’t one voice. It’s a choir. `c5_shack.log.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: Dad heard voices in the hum, and he wanted my ears on it. Was that the only reason they asked me along? **REV** `c5_shack.log.02`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: Dad’s last reel isn’t one hum. It’s many voices holding one note. “It isn’t one voice. It’s a choir.” Mom and Dad weren’t out on the bay for the view. They were investigating, and they wanted my ears.* **NEW** `c5_shack.log.find`

### Scene complete

- *Steve Mercer: Dad’s club is back on the air. And Mom’s notebook is in the KBWR record library, at the station that fired me.* **NEW** `c5_shack.recap.02`
- *Steve Mercer: Twice today someone stood on the path below the shack. Both times, by the time I looked, he was gone.* **NEW** `c5_shack.recap.03`

- **Steve Mercer**: I know where the notebook is. `c5_shack.out.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: And the KBWR tower is blinking. Five flashes, then dark. Last night it was six. **NEW** `c5_shack.out.03`
- **Steve Mercer**: Six days, Rook said. It’s counting down to the signing. **NEW** `c5_shack.out.04`

## Chapter 5: Two of him (`c5_station`)

**Where:** KBWR record library · **When:** Friday, November 2, night · **Present:** Steve Mercer, Stephen, DJ AutoRick

**Objective:** Get into the record library and find Mom’s notebook.

**Revision notes:** C30, critic C30, C22, U-relationships, U-narration, U-timeline (Halcyon bought KBWR a year ago; Steve was let go five weeks ago)

> Implementation: Stephen’s lines before he names himself show the speaker as “Steve Mercer?” (line-level display).

> Implementation: halcyon replaces the dated-stamps quiz with the early-night pattern: Halcyon sent Steve home on ninth nights so the tower could transmit unattended.

> Implementation: The steve narration between out.02 and out.03 carries the plot’s heart beat: Steve watching his own face learn his grief.

> Implementation: Small talk is with the radio host only. Stephen is hidden until the last lead, and a scene ends as soon as its last lead is solved, so a Stephen topic could never be heard.

### Lead 1: Unattended console (`c5_station.automation`) · rows · difficulty 2

**When the lead opens:**
- **Steve Mercer**: AutoRick’s console. I wrote half these carts myself. **NEW** `c5_station.automation.lead.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: The maintenance door only unlatches while my ident plays. Twelve seconds. But the walk over there takes fifteen. **NEW** `c5_station.automation.lead.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: And the news cart is locked. It only plays on the hour, and it’s twenty past. **NEW** `c5_station.automation.lead.03`

**Goal:** Get through the maintenance door into the record library.

**What Steve knows:** Steve built KBWR’s automation schedule before Halcyon replaced him with it. The maintenance door to the record library unlatches only while the station ident plays: twelve seconds, in Steve’s own voice.

**Prompt:** What should Steve queue, and where should he wait?

**Instruction:** Queue two carts so Steve is already at the maintenance door when the ident starts. WEATHER lasts 40 seconds, IDENT 12 seconds and NEWS 1 minute 30. NEWS only plays on the hour. The walk from the console to the door takes 15 seconds.
- Play first: ✓ WEATHER / IDENT / NEWS
- Play second: WEATHER / ✓ IDENT / NEWS
- Wait at: The console / ✓ The maintenance door

**Hints:**
1. Can Steve cross the room before the ident ends?
2. Something long has to play while he walks, and it can’t be the locked cart.
3. The ident lasts twelve seconds and the walk takes fifteen. Play something long enough to walk through, with the ident straight after it.

**On success:**
- **Steve Mercer**: I built this schedule before they replaced me with it. And the door still opens for my voice. **REV** `c5_station.automation.01`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: WEATHER gave me forty seconds to cross the room. Then my own voice, on the ident, unlatched the maintenance door.* **NEW** `c5_station.automation.find`

### Lead 2: First night at the desk (`c5_station.sleeve`) · board · difficulty 2
*Unlocks after:* c5_station.automation

**When the lead opens:**
- **Steve Mercer**: The record library. Every master we ever made, filed the way Dad taught me. **NEW** `c5_station.sleeve.lead.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: Mom never wrote my first show. She wrote my first night at the desk. The engineer sits at the desk. **NEW** `c5_station.sleeve.lead.02`

**Goal:** Find the sleeve where Helen hid her notebook.

**What Steve knows:** Helen’s slip said the notebook was “with the good stuff. Your first night at the desk.” KBWR files every master under its original broadcast, never under a repeat.

**Prompt:** Which sleeve did Mom mean?

**Instruction:** Follow Mom’s words through the duty book to the right sleeve.

**Case notes:**
1. On Tuesday the 4th, Steve sat at the engineer’s desk for the midnight show, and Jo presented.
2. On Tuesday the 11th, Steve presented at the microphone, and Jo sat at the desk.
3. On the 18th, Pat played a repeat of the show from the 4th.
4. KBWR files every master with its original broadcast, never with a repeat.
5. The blue sleeve holds the original show from the 4th, and the yellow sleeve holds the repeat.

**Stage 1 (match):** Put one card on each question.
- Cards: The 4th, The 11th, With the original broadcast, The blue sleeve
- Answer: Which was Steve’s first night at the desk? → The 4th; Where is that night’s master filed? → With the original broadcast; Which sleeve should Steve open? → The blue sleeve

**Hints:**
1. Mom wrote “at the desk”, not “at the microphone”.
2. Which Tuesday did Steve sit at the desk? Repeats are never filed as masters.
3. Find the desk night in the duty book, then the sleeve that holds that night’s original.

**On success:**
- **Steve Mercer** [under a pile of records]: I knocked the whole shelf over. The blue one landed in my lap. **NEW** `c5_station.sleeve.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: Mom’s handwriting. Mandarin shorthand. And this last page... I’ve never seen that script. `c5_station.sleeve.01`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: My first night at the desk was the 4th, when I engineered and Jo presented. The original is in the blue sleeve, and Mom’s notebook was in its back pocket. I can’t read a word of it.* **NEW** `c5_station.sleeve.find`

### Lead 3: Halcyon’s paperwork (`c5_station.halcyon`) · rows · difficulty 2
*Unlocks after:* c5_station.automation

**When the lead opens:**
- **Steve Mercer**: Halcyon Media Holdings. The company that fired me. Their filing is very tidy. **NEW** `c5_station.halcyon.lead.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: Three early nights in September. Halcyon said they were saving on my overtime. **NEW** `c5_station.halcyon.lead.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: And the tower log says the tower ran by itself on those same three nights. **NEW** `c5_station.halcyon.lead.03`

**Goal:** Find out why Halcyon really let Steve go.

**What Steve knows:** The office is full of paperwork from Halcyon Media Holdings, the company that bought KBWR a year ago and replaced Steve with AutoRick five weeks ago. Steve always thought it was about money.

**Prompt:** What do the duty sheets and the tower log say, side by side?

**Instruction:** Compare the dates. Halcyon sent Steve home at 23:30 on September 6, 15 and 24. The tower ran alone from 23:45 on those same nights. Steve’s last shift was September 28. Since then, the tower ran alone on October 3, 12, 21 and 30.
- Steve’s early nights were: Every Friday / ✓ Every ninth night / Whenever ratings dropped
- The tower transmits alone: Every night since the sale / ✓ Only once the building is empty / Whenever AutoRick reads the news
- Next unattended night: November 3 / November 7 / ✓ November 8

**Hints:**
1. Count the days from September 6 to September 15.
2. The tower only ran alone after Steve had gone home, never while he was at work.
3. The gap between the dates is the same every time. Count it once more from October 30.

**On success:**
- **Steve Mercer**: They needed the tower, and they needed the building empty. I was the last person here. `c5_station.halcyon.01`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: Halcyon sent me home early every ninth night, and the tower transmitted alone as soon as I left. Then they let me go for good. The next unattended night is November 8.* **NEW** `c5_station.halcyon.find`

### Lead 4: The man with my face (`c5_station.stephen`) · board · difficulty 2
*Unlocks after:* c5_station.sleeve, c5_station.halcyon

**When the lead opens:**
- **Steve Mercer?**: That cardigan. Is this what happens when I stop trying? **MOV** `c5_station.in.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: ...The man Carmen saw at the market. My face. **NEW** `c5_station.stephen.lead.01`
- **Steve Mercer?**: It’s a good face. You’re not doing it any favors. **NEW** `c5_station.stephen.lead.02`
- **Steve Mercer?**: The last thing I remember of home is breakfast in Dad’s shack. Mom’s jam jar, with the chip in the blue lid. **NEW** `c5_station.stephen.lead.03`
- **Steve Mercer?**: A tape that says Mom, last call? Mom never leaves voicemails. She calls back. **NEW** `c5_station.stephen.lead.04`

**Goal:** Find out how far the other man’s memory reaches, without feeding him the answers.

**What Steve knows:** The man with Steve’s face remembers breakfast with Mom and Dad. He seems to know nothing about the wanted posters. Steve lays three things on the table and lets him talk first, the way June would.

**Prompt:** Where does his memory end?

**Instruction:** Sort each thing by when he could have learned it.

**Case notes:**
1. Before he sees anything, he describes breakfast in Dad’s shack: Mom’s jam jar, with a chip in the blue lid.
2. He looks at the cassette labelled MOM, LAST CALL, and shrugs. “Mom never leaves voicemails. She calls back.”
3. The wanted poster lies face down. He can read it only after Steve turns it over.
4. Mom’s last call came from the boat, the night after that breakfast.

**Stage 1 (match):** Put one card on each question.
- Cards: The chipped blue lid, Mom’s last call, The wanted poster, Steve’s first show
- Answer: What does he remember on his own? → The chipped blue lid; What can’t he place? → Mom’s last call; What did he only learn tonight? → The wanted poster

**Hints:**
1. Which detail did he give before Steve showed him anything?
2. He shrugged at the tape. He read the poster only after Steve turned it over.
3. Sort by when he could know it: before he walked in, not at all, or after Steve showed him tonight.

**On success:**
- **Steve Mercer?**: Sixty-one percent. That’s what the attendant said. I assumed the rest was a compliment. `c5_station.stephen.01`
- **Steve Mercer?**: The tank slate said sixty-one was the memory index, not sixty-one percent of a person. Last stable binding: breakfast in Dad’s shack. **NEW** `c5_station.stephen.03`
- **Stephen**: Call me Stephen. One of us should sound like a grown-up. **NEW** `c5_station.stephen.04`
- **Steve Mercer**: Sit down. There’s something you need to know. `c5_station.stephen.02`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: He remembers Mom’s chipped jam jar, and nothing of home after that breakfast. He doesn’t know about the boat. He doesn’t know they’re gone.* **NEW** `c5_station.stephen.find`

- *Steve Mercer: And the man with my face had a sixty-one-percent memory index, but his last stable binding ended at breakfast before the boat. Eighteen months of my life were missing.* **NEW** `c5_station.recap.02`

## Chapter 6: Iris (`c6_iris`)

**Where:** Bellwether, the market and the pier · **When:** Monday, November 5, morning · **Present:** Steve Mercer, Stephen, Nora’s copy, DJ AutoRick

**Chapter card:** Chapter 6, *Iris* · Monday morning · Steve wants to free Calder and learn what the Veyri want.

**Objective:** Meet the stranger who warned you about Rook. Keep the town looking somewhere else.

**Coach tip:** Line the clocks up by the radio first. The meeting on the pier waits for Stephen’s sighting at the market.

**Revision notes:** C28, C29, C36, C22, U-relationships, U-narration

> Implementation: Iris’s turn at the waterline on the 30th (Chapter 3 stakeout) was her second missed re-sync, on purpose. The first, on the 21st, was an accident. She says so in the takes lead.

> Implementation: New sound assets for hold: effect.halcyon_hold_seattle and effect.halcyon_hold_denver. Different clerks, different ring counts, the same eight bars of hold music ending on the same note.

> Implementation: The text message line uses display_text to show the message as typed.

> Implementation: Iris is shown as “Nora’s copy” until out.03, where she names herself. From c6_jail on she is “Iris”.

> Implementation: Switch to Stephen to arrange his sighting at Gus’s bread window. When he leaves for the market, control returns to Steve. Iris is not on the pier until the alibi is done.

> Implementation: The outro is Iris’s account of Sunday night: the crew retook her from the otter house for a reset, left her on a laundry cart in the Gallery, and she set a slow, reversible wake on Nora’s, Lyle Boone’s and Dale’s beds before riding out with the wash. This is how Nora wakes in Interlude 2.

> Implementation: Iris then clears Steve of Nora’s abduction in front of the market. The search for Nora ends, but Steve is still wanted for fleeing federal custody.

- *Steve Mercer: Stephen and I spent the weekend in Dad’s shack, arguing about haircuts. Every night at nine, he told the earpiece I’d gone north.* **NEW** `c6_iris.ctx.01`
- *Steve Mercer: On Monday morning my flip phone buzzed.* **NEW** `c6_iris.ctx.02`
- **DJ AutoRick** (broadcast): Police are seeking Steve Mercer, last seen wearing a cardigan. Yesterday he was sunny. **NEW** `c6_iris.in.03`
- **Stephen**: I’m wearing the cardigan. **NEW** `c6_iris.in.04`
- **Steve Mercer**: Take it off. **NEW** `c6_iris.in.05`
- **Steve Mercer** [reading the text aloud]: “pier. 8:10. come alone. please.” No capitals. Same as the warning about Rook. *(spoken: “Pier. Eight-ten. Come alone. Please.” No capitals. Same as the warning about Rook.)* **NEW** `c6_iris.in.06`
- **Stephen**: You hide. I’ll be charming in public. We should each use our strengths. `c6_iris.in.01`

### Lead 1: Market corner (`c6_iris.alibi`) · board · difficulty 2 · played as Stephen

**When the lead opens:**
- **Stephen**: Three errands, three clocks. Nobody in this town agrees what time it is. **NEW** `c6_iris.alibi.lead.01`
- **Stephen**: Gus sets his oven clock four minutes fast. He says it keeps the bread honest. **NEW** `c6_iris.alibi.lead.02`
- **Stephen**: The bus clock runs two minutes slow. The florist sets hers by the radio, like a sensible person. **NEW** `c6_iris.alibi.lead.03`
- **Stephen**: And Gus has my poster in his window. He asks everyone to help him fix the smudge. **NEW** `c6_iris.alibi.lead.04`

**Goal:** Give the town a sighting of Steve’s face across town at exactly 8:10.

**What Steve knows:** At 8:10 Steve meets the texter on the pier. If someone in town sees Steve’s face somewhere else at that same minute, the search goes there instead. Stephen has three errands, and each one runs on a different clock.

**Prompt:** Which errand puts Stephen’s face in front of a witness at exactly 8:10 by the radio?

**Instruction:** Put each slip on radio time, then pick the witness who will really see Stephen’s face.

**Case notes:**
1. Gus’s oven clock runs four minutes fast.
2. The bus clock runs two minutes slow, and the florist’s clock matches the radio.
3. The slips read BREAD 08:14, BUS 08:05 and FLOWERS 08:12.
4. The bus driver stamps fares without looking up, and the florist takes orders by phone.
5. Gus has a smudged wanted poster in his window, and he asks customers to help him fix it.

**Stage 1 (match):** Put one card on each question.
- Cards: 08:10, 08:07, 08:12, Gus at the bread window, The bus driver
- Answer: Bread slip, by the radio → 08:10; Bus slip, by the radio → 08:07; Flowers slip, by the radio → 08:12; Who will look at Stephen’s face? → Gus at the bread window

**Hints:**
1. Use the radio as the one true clock.
2. Take four minutes off Gus’s slip, and add two minutes to the bus slip.
3. Only one slip lands on 08:10, and only one witness looks a customer in the face.

**On success:**
- **Stephen**: Good morning. Yes, that’s my face on the poster. Terrible photograph. **REV** `c6_iris.alibi.01`
- *Steve Mercer: By the time an officer reached the bread window, Stephen was out through the alley.* **NEW** `c6_iris.alibi.02`

**Finding:**

## Chapter 6: Two Sheriffs (`c6_jail`)

**Where:** Sheriff’s office and jail · **When:** Monday, November 5, afternoon · **Present:** Steve Mercer, Stephen, Pell, Sheriff June Calder, Iris, Gus Albano

**Objective:** Find the cell key, then work out which June is the real one.

**Revision notes:** C28, critic C28, C22, U-relationships, U-narration

> Implementation: Both Junes use the calder voice. Line-level display tells them apart: “June on the radio” (her copy, out on the harbor, heard through the front-desk radio) and “June in cell two”.

> Implementation: Pell names himself in the intro, so the display rule switches from “Dale Kittredge” to “Pell” in this scene.

> Implementation: Steve visits alone. Stephen and Iris wait behind Gus’s bakery. Lyle’s copy is out on the harbor with June’s copy, so the deputy never sees Steve and has nothing to report.

> Implementation: The crossword test pays off Chapter 3: a fresh private fact, checked on paper.

> Implementation: Art (optional): Gus’s bakery van with a giant croissant on its roof, at the loading door, for the departure.

### Opening
- *Steve Mercer: The Sheriff’s office, just before four. Since Iris spoke up at the market, the search for Nora was over. June’s copy and Lyle’s copy were out on the harbor, calling the boats in.* **NEW** `c6_jail.ctx.01`
- *Steve Mercer: Stephen and Iris waited behind Gus’s bakery next door. I walked in the front door. Pell was sorting the afternoon mail.* **NEW** `c6_jail.ctx.02`
- **June on the radio** (radio) [over the front-desk radio, from the harbor]: All boats, stand down. Nora Reyes is safe. The search is closed. **NEW** `c6_jail.in.04`
- **Pell**: I am required to report your location. `c6_jail.in.01`

- **Steve Mercer**: Maybe we should put the dog in charge. **REV** `c6_jail.in.03`
- **Pell**: My name is Pell. Dale is asleep on the ship. I have worn his face for a year. **NEW** `c6_jail.in.05`

### Lead 1: Pell’s sorting case (`c6_jail.mail`) · board · difficulty 1 · played as Steve Mercer

**When the lead opens:**
- **Pell**: The cell key was returned to the post this morning. I filed it. **NEW** `c6_jail.mail.lead.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: Where? **NEW** `c6_jail.mail.lead.02`
- **Pell**: Correctly. **NEW** `c6_jail.mail.lead.03`
- **Pell**: I file by destination. The carbon says Jail, returned key. That is register entry eighteen. **NEW** `c6_jail.mail.lead.04`
- **Pell**: Each slot holds two entries. Slot one holds entries one and two, and so on. **NEW** `c6_jail.mail.lead.05`
- **Pell**: County rule. When a cell is used, the spare key goes back to the clerk by registered post. The Sheriff wrote that rule. Her copy follows it exactly. **NEW** `c6_jail.mail.lead.06`

**Goal:** Find the cell key in Pell’s sorting case.

**What Steve knows:** Pell files everything exactly by the rules. His sorting case has three drawers, labelled Counter, Returns and Jail. The jail’s cell key went back through the post this morning.

**Prompt:** Route the returned key through Pell’s case, then use the same delivery to reach June privately.

**Instruction:** Follow the labels on the carbon and register strip. Finish with the handoff only the woman in cell two can receive.

**Case notes:**
1. Return carbon: ‘Destination JAIL · spare cell key · register 18.’ Pell files by destination, not by parcel status.
2. Register strip: slot 8 holds entries 15–16; slot 9 holds 17–18; slot 10 holds 19–20.
3. June’s own crossword is in the jail pile. A marked square posted through cell two reaches the prisoner without going over the radio.

**Stage 1 (route):** Trace the key and the private question.
- Cards: Jail, Returns, Slot 9, Slot 10, Marked crossword through cell two
- Answer: Destination drawer → Jail; Register pocket → Slot 9; Private handoff → Marked crossword through cell two

**Hints:**
1. Pell files by where a thing is going, not by what happened to it.
2. The register strip prints entry 18 beside slot 9; there is nothing to calculate.
3. Jail drawer, slot 9, then post June’s marked crossword through cell two.

**On success:**
- **Pell**: Correctly delivered. Biscuit may inspect the chain of custody. `c6_jail.mail.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: June’s own crossword is in the jail pile. I’ll circle one square and post it through her slot. **NEW** `c6_jail.mail.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: Twenty-three across, where it crosses eight down. Only the woman in cell two will see that square. **NEW** `c6_jail.mail.03`
- **Steve Mercer**: Then I’ll call the Sheriff on the desk radio. Both Junes get the same question. **NEW** `c6_jail.mail.04`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: The key was in the Jail drawer, slot 9, where entries 17 and 18 share a slot. And on June’s crossword I circled the square where 23 Across crosses 8 Down. Only the woman in cell two will see it.* **NEW** `c6_jail.mail.find`

### Lead 2: The two Junes (`c6_jail.crossword`) · rows · difficulty 2 · played as Steve Mercer
*Unlocks after:* c6_jail.mail

**When the lead opens:**
- **June on the radio** (radio) [over the front-desk radio, from the harbor]: Mercer. That woman in cell two is a copy. Don’t you dare let her out. **NEW** `c6_jail.crossword.lead.01`
- **June in cell two** [through the cell door]: Check my paperwork, Mercer. I taught you that. **NEW** `c6_jail.crossword.lead.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: I’ll ask them both which letter I circled today. Only one of them has seen it. **NEW** `c6_jail.crossword.lead.03`

**Goal:** Check Iris’s story: which June is locked in cell two, and which one is out on the harbor?

**What Steve knows:** June’s rule: memories can’t prove who you are, so you check the paperwork. Iris says the crew scanned June at the safe house. Her copy runs the office, and the real June has been locked in cell two since Friday. Steve posted June’s crossword, with one square circled, into cell two and nowhere else. Whoever is in that cell has seen it. Anyone else has to guess.

**Prompt:** The June in cell two answers on a slip of paper. The June on the radio answers out loud. What should Steve ask about the square he personally circled, and whose answer should he accept?

**Instruction:** Ask both Junes only: “Which letter did I circle?” Keep the crossing to yourself. Use Steve’s notebook to check their answers. 23 Across, ‘beyond imitation’, is INIMITABLE, shown as I _ I _ I _ A _ L _. Its first, second and last blanks cross 3 Down, 8 Down and 12 Down.
- Privately check the crossing Steve circled: 17 Across and 8 Down / 23 Across and 3 Down / ✓ 23 Across and 8 Down
- Accept the June who says: ✓ M / T / A

**Hints:**
1. Steve said which square he circled when he found the key. His notebook has it too.
2. Write INIMITABLE under the blanks, one letter at a time.
3. Find the blank that crosses the down clue Steve chose, and read the letter there.

**On success:**
- **Steve Mercer**: Radio June said T. Close. Wrong. **NEW** `c6_jail.crossword.03`
- **June in cell two**: Twenty-three across. Inimitable. Unlike my colleague on the radio. **REV** `c6_jail.crossword.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: June. We’re getting you out. `c6_jail.crossword.02`
- **June on the radio** (radio): Stay right there, Mercer. I’m on my way in. **NEW** `c6_jail.crossword.04`
- **June in cell two**: She knows my files. She doesn’t know me. Back door, Mercer. **NEW** `c6_jail.crossword.05`
- *Steve Mercer: Pell held the back door. When her patrol car pulled up out front, we were already behind Gus’s bakery.* **NEW** `c6_jail.crossword.06`

**Finding:**
- *Steve Mercer: The June in cell two had my crossword in her hand: the M where 23 Across crosses 8 Down. The June on the radio guessed a T, so she has never been near that cell. Iris was right. I unlocked cell two, and we left by the back before her copy reached the front.* **NEW** `c6_jail.crossword.find`

*Removed leads:* c6_jail.distract, c6_jail.exit

- *Steve Mercer: Five fugitives and a beagle. We needed a car nobody would look for.* **NEW** `c6_jail.recap.02`
- *Steve Mercer: Stephen and Iris were waiting behind Gus’s bakery next door, where nobody would see our faces.* **NEW** `c6_jail.recap.03`

## Interlude 2: Nobody is coming to fix this (`i2_nora`)

**Where:** The Gallery · **When:** Monday night, inside the Long Patience · **Present:** Nora Reyes, Dale Kittredge, Lyle Boone

**Chapter card:** Interlude, *Nora* · Inside the Long Patience · Nora wakes up on the ship. Nobody is coming to fix this.

**Objective:** Steady the others, learn their names and find a way through the Gallery.

**Coach tip:** You are Nora, awake on the ship. The guard walks a regular lap. Count it before you move.

**Revision notes:** C37, C22, U-relationships, U-narration

> Implementation: In the Iris chapter, Iris tells Steve how she set this wake: on Sunday night the crew retook her for a reset and left her on a laundry cart in the Gallery, and she set a slow wake on three beds before riding out with the wash. This interlude begins when the slow cycle finishes Monday night.

> Implementation: The lids remain shut while the beds support Nora, Lyle and Dale. They open only after all three breathe independently.

> Implementation: This wake is reversible and leaves the mirror links active. The crew can put Nora back under; Iris’s permanent link-break remains a Chapter 9 choice.

> Implementation: jobs keeps the case board with a single stage (the route and logging stages are cut).

> Implementation: The eye under the frost is narrated in the departure. Art (optional): an insert of Row Zero with one enormous eye open under cracked frost.

> Implementation: Nora plays this interlude, so her small talk is with Dale and Lyle, the two sleepers awake beside her.

### Opening
- *Nora Reyes: I woke up in amber light, in a bed I didn’t choose, in a hall that went on forever.* **NEW** `i2_nora.ctx.01`
- *Nora Reyes: The bed panel said Monday, November fifth. That was how I learned five months had passed.* **NEW** `i2_nora.ctx.02`
- *Nora Reyes: I was frightened for about a minute. Then I did what I do at any party where nobody knows each other. I started learning names.* **NEW** `i2_nora.ctx.03`
- **Nora Reyes**: I can move my hand. That’s a start. `i2_nora.in.01`
- **Dale Kittredge**: If this is a hospital, I’m changing hospitals. `i2_nora.in.02`
- **Nora Reyes**: Names first. Complaints after we have a plan. `i2_nora.in.03`
- *Nora Reyes: The glass stayed shut while three blue strips counted our breaths. The beds did the work until each of us could breathe alone.* **NEW** `i2_nora.in.04`
- *Nora Reyes: Only then did the latches release. WAKE COMPLETE, the panel said. MIRROR LINK ACTIVE.* **NEW** `i2_nora.in.05`

**What Steve knows:** A guard walks the Gallery in laps. Nora counts under her breath and scratches marks on her bed rail.

**Instruction:** Step out when the far door shuts, and reach cover before the boots come back. Lap one: boots at 2, pump at 5, far door shuts at 8. The boots come back at 14, and the lap repeats. Crossing takes four counts.

- Reach cover at count: 21 / ✓ 24 / 27

3. The lap is twelve counts long. Find the next time the door shuts after 14, then add four.

**On success:**
- **Lyle Boone**: I can keep that latch from catching. `i2_nora.jobs.01`
- **Dale Kittredge**: I’ll remember the turns. `i2_nora.jobs.02`
- **Nora Reyes**: Good. I’ll handle the pump. `i2_nora.jobs.03`

**Finding:**
- *Nora Reyes: Lyle keeps the hatch from catching. Dale remembers every turn. I take the pumps. Water is the one thing on this ship I understand.* **NEW** `i2_nora.jobs.find`

### Lead 3: Gallery circulation (`i2_nora.pump`) · rows · difficulty 2
*Unlocks after:* i2_nora.jobs

**When the lead opens:**
- **Nora Reyes**: Three service branches. Let’s see what each one does. **NEW** `i2_nora.pump.lead.01`
- **Nora Reyes**: The top dial reads an empty wash line. Ignore it. Watch the return and the bed flow. **NEW** `i2_nora.pump.lead.02`
- **Nora Reyes**: Bed flow has to stay at three or more. The return has to drop below three. **NEW** `i2_nora.pump.lead.03`

**Goal:** Lower the pressure at the hatch without starving the beds.

**What Steve knows:** Nora knows pumps from the aquarium. The Gallery runs on water too: one circuit feeds the beds, another returns it. Opening the hatch with the return pressure high would wake the whole hall.

**Prompt:** Which branch should Nora open, and which dial should she trust?

**Instruction:** Pick the branch that keeps bed flow at 3 or more and drops RETURN below 3. Amber: upper 4, RETURN 7, bed flow 4. Blue: upper 1, RETURN 2, bed flow 4. White: upper 3, RETURN 2, bed flow 1.
- Branch: Amber / White / ✓ Blue
- Dial to trust: Upper / ✓ Lower RETURN

**Hints:**
1. The upper dial reads an empty line. Watch the RETURN dial and the bed flow.
2. Amber keeps the beds fed but the RETURN is high. White drops the pressure but starves the beds.
3. Ignore the upper dial. You need bed flow of 3 or more and a RETURN below 3. Only one trial does both.

**On success:**
- **Nora Reyes**: Pressure down. Nobody’s breathing changes. That’s our route. `i2_nora.pump.01`

**Finding:**
- *Nora Reyes: The Blue branch keeps the beds at 4 and drops the RETURN to 2. The hatch can open, and nobody’s breathing changes.* **NEW** `i2_nora.pump.find`

### Lead 4: Gallery intercom (`i2_nora.message`) · choice · difficulty 2
*Unlocks after:* i2_nora.pump

**When the lead opens:**
- **Nora Reyes**: If he comes. When he comes. He’ll come. **NEW** `i2_nora.message.lead.01`

**Goal:** Leave a message for Steve, in case he ever comes.

**What Steve knows:** The Gallery intercom has three message slots. If Steve comes at all, he might come on any watch. Only Steve should hear what Nora says.

**Prompt:** Where should Nora leave her message for Steve?

- Slot A: plays in every bed now. *Wrong:* Every sleeper, and every guard, would hear it right now.
- ✓ Slot B: waits by this bed for PLAY
- Slot C: clears when the watch changes. *Wrong:* It would be gone by the next watch, and Steve could come on any watch.

**Hints:**
1. Who should hear it, and when?
2. It has to survive a change of watch, and only Steve should start it.
3. Rule out the slot every guard would hear and the slot that clears at the change of watch.

**On success:**
- **Nora Reyes** (private_recording): Nora records a private message for Steve. *(spoken: Steve, if you ever hear this message: I am furious with you. I love you. Please stop making me do both alone.)* `i2_nora.message.01`

**Finding:**
- *Nora Reyes: My message for Steve is in Slot B, by my bed. It will wait for him.* **NEW** `i2_nora.message.find`

### Scene complete
- *Nora Reyes: I know every name in my row now. I have a route, a crew and a pump that behaves.* **NEW** `i2_nora.recap.01`
- *Nora Reyes: I counted thirteen months of silence before they took me. Five more went by while I slept. I still want to hear what he has to say.* **NEW** `i2_nora.recap.02`

### Departure
- **Nora Reyes**: Helen. Frank. Steve is going to be so glad. `i2_nora.out.01`
- **Nora Reyes**: Mrs. Penhallow? Row three. I should remember that. `i2_nora.out.02`
- **Nora Reyes** *(sound: wick)*: And you, under the frost... you can hear me, can’t you? `i2_nora.out.03`
- *Nora Reyes: Under the frost, something opened one enormous eye. It looked at me. Then it closed again.* **NEW** `i2_nora.out.04`

**Next:** Chapter 7 (c7_gate).

## Chapter 7: The President’s Other Appointment (`c7_gate`)

**Where:** Sentinel Point gate · **When:** Wednesday, November 7, morning · **Present:** Steve Mercer, Stephen, Pell, Rook’s copy, President Elena Ward, Dr. Evelyn Shaw

**Chapter card:** Chapter 7, *The President’s Other Appointment* · The day of the signing · Steve wants to stop the swap of the President.

**Objective:** Get two Steves and a mail carrier into the signing. Watch the President.

**Coach tip:** Three of you today: Stephen, Pell and Steve. Steve’s face is on every wanted poster, so he stays out of sight until you are through the gate. Each lead shows who should handle it. Press Q or use the name menu at the top right to switch.

**Revision notes:** C14, critic C14 (left wrist), C22, U-relationships, U-narration, U-cast (no new gate_guard voice: Rook’s copy works the gate)

> Implementation: C14 asked for a new gate_guard role. Rook’s copy runs the FBI liaison at the event (plot), so he works the checkpoint instead. No new voice is needed.

> Implementation: badge and post become rows puzzles (drop the case boards). truck stays a choice with a new prompt.

> Implementation: Ward’s ribbon burn on her left wrist is the clue that solves c7_swap.map. Keep ‘left wrist’ in every mention.

### Opening
- *Steve Mercer: Sentinel Point. An old antenna field in the fog north of town, flags on every mast, and the President of the United States due at noon.* **NEW** `c7_gate.ctx.01`
- *Steve Mercer: We spent Tuesday in the back room of Gus’s bakery, planning on flour sacks.* **NEW** `c7_gate.in.03`
- *Steve Mercer: We couldn’t call anyone. The FBI’s man at the signing was Rook’s copy, and Rook’s list had names in Washington.* **NEW** `c7_gate.in.04`
- *Steve Mercer: So we used what we had. Halcyon runs the sound for the signing, Stephen still has their crew pass, and Dad’s tape has the tone.* **NEW** `c7_gate.in.05`
- *Steve Mercer: June wrote to the President in her own hand. Pell would deliver it the way he delivers everything: into the right hand, on time.* **NEW** `c7_gate.in.06`
- *Steve Mercer: Gus had two catering passes for the press tent. June and Iris would use them at the red window, at ten past eleven, on foot, in his aprons and hairnets, carrying croissants.* **NEW** `c7_gate.in.07`

### Small talk
**The fog** · Stephen · after `c7_gate.badge`
- **Steve Mercer**: Can you see the trucks from here? I can barely see my own feet. **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.stephen.1.01`
- **Stephen**: Good. Fog is very flattering. Nobody looks like a wanted poster in fog. **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.stephen.1.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: Dad used to say fog makes every sound feel closer. **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.stephen.1.03`
- **Stephen**: He said it on the boat, every summer. I remember that one. I think I do. **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.stephen.1.04`

**Breakfast** · Stephen
- **Stephen**: I had two croissants from Gus this morning. He gave me a third one for free. **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.stephen.2.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: He knows you’re not me, Stephen. He just likes you better. **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.stephen.2.02`
- **Stephen**: He said the poster doesn’t do me justice. He’s right, you know. **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.stephen.2.03`

**Saren’s hunter** · Stephen · after `c7_gate.badge`
- **Steve Mercer**: Rook’s copy wished you luck finding me. **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.stephen.3.01`
- **Stephen**: I only told him the truth. You were very close. You were right under the sound desk. **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.stephen.3.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: Please stop enjoying this so much. **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.stephen.3.03`
- **Stephen**: I can’t. This is the most fun I’ve had in my whole short life. **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.stephen.3.04`

**Biscuit** · Pell · after `c7_gate.badge`
- **Steve Mercer**: Who is looking after Biscuit today? **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.pell.1.01`
- **Pell**: The Sheriff’s neighbor. I left written instructions. Four pages, with diagrams. **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.pell.1.02`
- **Steve Mercer**: Four pages, for one beagle? **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.pell.1.03`
- **Pell**: She is a very important beagle. She has a schedule. **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.pell.1.04`

**The mail route** · Pell · after `c7_gate.badge`
- **Pell**: Dale’s route has two hundred and twelve mailboxes. I know every one of them. **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.pell.2.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: Do you actually like the work? **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.pell.2.02`
- **Pell**: I like the dogs, and the people who say thank you. Mostly the dogs. **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.pell.2.03`

**Gate coffee** · Rook’s copy · after `c7_gate.badge`
- **Rook’s copy**: The coffee in the press tent is terrible. I’ve had four cups. **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.rook_copy.1.01`
- **Stephen**: You don’t seem to mind it very much. **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.rook_copy.1.02`

**The one he was made from** · Rook’s copy · after `c7_gate.badge`
- **Rook’s copy**: Do you ever think about him? The one you were made from? **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.rook_copy.2.01`
- **Stephen**: Every single day. He snores, apparently. **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.rook_copy.2.02`
- **Rook’s copy**: I wonder if mine dreams. Down there, in the cold. **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.rook_copy.2.03`

**The old truck** · Dr. Evelyn Shaw · after `c7_gate.truck.02`
- **Dr. Evelyn Shaw**: This truck is older than you are. I rewired half of it myself. **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.shaw.1.01`
- **Steve Mercer**: It still sounds good, though. **NEW** `c7_gate.talk.shaw.1.02`
